# Fragment issues — section 5b, rows 401 to 425

> Rows 401 to 425 of section 5b of [the register](../FRAGMENT-ISSUES.md), in their own file since 2026-10-09 so that
> they can be read alone. **The section's own rules are on that page, under its heading.** A new
> row goes at the end of the section's last file, whatever its number, and the next run of
> [`tools/split-register.py`](../../tools/split-register.py) moves it to the file its number
> belongs to. [`tools/register-text.py`](../../tools/register-text.py) reads every row back into
> the register for every tool that reads it, so a row here is seen exactly as it was seen there.

| # | Defect | State |
|---|---|---|
| **401** | ***"how many rooms are there"* WAS ANSWERED BY `PLACE_ROOMS`, A CHANGE THAT PLACES ROOMS, ON THE TRAINED BACKEND - THE ROOM TWIN OF [ROW 355](#)'S SPACES, AND MOST OTHER WAYS OF ASKING FOR A ROOM COUNT STILL REACH A CHANGE ON ONE BACKEND OR THE OTHER.** Reported to this session on 2026-10-09 and reproduced on scratch stores from `main` at `aebca7ad`, 480 fragments, through `heron_brain.lookup` - the function `heron_lookup` calls - behind the 2024 wall, on both backends: lexical, and `model` (`model2vec` 0.9.0, `minishlab/potion-base-8M`, in a venv outside the repository, warmed and checked before the first question). **On `model`:** `PLACE_ROOMS` (FRG-GEO-014, DRAFT, MODIFY), then `SELECT_UNENCLOSED_ROOMS`, `REPORT_AREA_VOLUME_COMPUTATIONS`, `CHECK_ROOM_MEP_COMPLETENESS` and `COUNT_BY_SPATIAL_CONTAINER` - and none of the five counts rooms. A question answered by a write is the one routing outcome D-86 does not leave to judgement. **On lexical:** `REPORT_AREA_VOLUME_COMPUTATIONS` (FRG-GEO-056, READ), which reads one document setting and counts nothing. It was `COUNT_BY_SPATIAL_CONTAINER` on `be933bf2` and moved with PR #460 ([row 387](#)), read to read, before PR #450 ([row 376](#)) - which expected to move it - had merged; with #450 merged locally it stays where it is. **Which read answers it, from the cards:** not `COUNT_BY_SPATIAL_CONTAINER` - it counts the elements handed in, broken down by the room that contains each: *how many diffusers in each room*, not how many rooms there are. Not `COUNT_ELEMENTS` or `REPORT_ROOM_SPACE_DATA`: both take a set of elements and refuse when nothing is selected (row 355). Not `REPORT_AREA_VOLUME_COMPUTATIONS`. It is `FILTER_ELEMENTS_BY_CATEGORY` (FRG-ELE-001, PROVEN, READ) with category `Rooms` and levelId none - the read row 355 chose for *how many MEP spaces are in the model*, and the filter half of the `count-elements` skill. Its collector has no placement test, so a room that was never placed is counted with the rest. | **FIXED FOR THIS SENTENCE - measured on the search, not run in Revit. THE KIND IS OPEN, as row 355's is.** The filter declares *how many rooms are there* with a dated comment, and both routing tables - the filter's and `PLACE_ROOMS`' - name it. Utterances and comments only: `impl/` is untouched, so the proof fingerprint (`492d8e1fd3344327`, implementation only) holds and the card stays PROVEN. Measured before and after on scratch stores, both backends, on `main` and on `main` with #450 merged: the sentence now answers by identity, to the filter, on all four. **What else moved, of 65 sentences** - room counts, room jobs on the edges, and 30 counts and lists from other domains: on lexical, *how many spaces are there* and *how many spaces are in this model* leave `REPORT_AREA_VOLUME_COMPUTATIONS` for the filter, read to read, and both are now right; on `model`, nothing. **Of 20 ways of asking for a room count** (the first ten written before the card was touched), 6 reach a change on lexical before and after, and 7 then 6 on `model`. The one other move is on `model`: *how many rooms on each level* goes from `SET_ROOM_LIMITS` to `PLACE_ROOMS`, a write to a write, with `REPORT_ROOM_SPACE_DATA` third both times. The new sentence shares *how many rooms* and puts the filter at words #2, so both writes drop one rank there, from an exact fused tie (words #2 + nearness #3 against #3 + #2) to a hair apart (#3 + #3 against #4 + #2). **Still reaching a change, unchanged by this:** *count the rooms* and *count all the rooms* (`SET_ROOM_LIMITS`, lexical); *how many rooms on level 2* (`SET_ROOM_LIMITS`, both); *how many rooms are on this floor* (`CREATE_FROM_ROOM_BOUNDARIES`, both); *how many rooms are there in the model* (`PLACE_ROOMS`, `model`); *tell me how many rooms this model has* and *are there any rooms in this model* (`PLACE_ROOMS`, both); *how many rooms on each level* (`model`). **Seen while measuring, not this row's to fix:** *list the rooms* and *show me all the rooms* reach `PLACE_ROOMS` on both backends; *which rooms are missing services*, which `COUNT_BY_SPATIAL_CONTAINER`'s routing table sends to `CHECK_ROOM_MEP_COMPLETENESS`, reaches `PLACE_ROOMS` on both; on `model`, *how many walls are there* reaches `SET_WALL_CONSTRAINTS` and *how many views are there* `SET_VIEW_PROPERTIES`. The kind stays open for row 355's reason: `PLACE_ROOMS`' card sits nearest any question about rooms or spaces, and the fix is a re-design of it measured on both backends, or reopening row 109's "warn, don't re-rank" - the owner's call, not a wording tweak. **Gates, before and after, both backends:** `check-routing` - the same exit, no question answered by a write, the claimed-and-not-reached list unchanged (21 lexical, 19 `model`), one new contest - this sentence on ranking alone, the filter #7 - and two rank ripples of one; `check-risk-crossings`, `score-routing` (the owner's questions) and `check-skill-routing` - the same answer question for question; `check-intrusion` - the same exit, `PLACE_ROOMS` in one more shortlist on `model` (67 to 68), and on lexical the twelfth place is `SET_AIR_TERMINAL_FLOW` at 50 shortlists where it was `DIMENSION_ROOMS` at 50. **Repeated on `main` `36f02b54`** (481 fragments, after #455, #459 and #450 merged) against this branch merged into it, both backends: the same answers and the same moves in all 77 probe sentences; the five tools move as they did on `aebca7ad`, except that `check-routing`'s two rank ripples are already on that `main`, so this adds none; on lexical `check-intrusion`'s twelfth place is `SET_AIR_TERMINAL_FLOW` at 49 where `main` has `DIMENSION_ROOMS` at 50; and the eleven CI gates exit 0 on the merged tree. **Repeated again on `main` `a2e00c7a`** (484 fragments, after #443, #445, #446, #449, #465 and #466) against a trial merge of this branch, lexical in a clean venv and `model` in one without the re-ranker the owner's laptop now runs live: the sentence answers by identity to the filter on both; on lexical *how many spaces are there* moves to the filter (that `main` already sends *how many spaces are in this model* there); on `model` *how many rooms on each level* goes from `SET_ROOM_LIMITS` to `PLACE_ROOMS`, as before; of the 20 room counts, 6 reach a change on lexical before and after, and 8 then 7 on `model`; `check-routing`'s ripples there are *find all elements of a category* #7 to #10 and *set the room number* #48 to #47, and the other four tools move as before; the eleven CI gates exit 0 on the trial merge. An identity answer comes before any ranking, so the re-ranker cannot change this sentence's answer; what it does to the others was not measured. **Two new crossings on that `main`, the same with and without this change:** *how high are these rooms* reaches `PLACE_ROOMS` on lexical, where `36f02b54` gave `SELECT_UNENCLOSED_ROOMS`, and *how many rooms have been placed* reaches `CENTER_ROOM_TAGS`, a write, on `model`, where it gave `REPORT_DOOR_ROOM_LINKS`. The live `heron_lookup` reads the main checkout's cards, so its answer changes once this is merged and that checkout is brought level. |
| **402** | **`tests/test_mcp_serves.py` DOES NOT RETURN INSIDE `check-gaps`' 300-SECOND CEILING IN A CLOUD CONTAINER, SO `check-gaps` LISTS IT UNFINISHED.** Found 2026-10-09 running `python tools/check-gaps.py` end to end for the first time in the [project review](../work-notes/plans/project-review-2026-10-08.md) (at low priority, beside two other agents' builds, on 4 CPUs): *HUNG test_mcp_serves.py - still running after 300s, gave up*. Timed side by side under the same load: `main` at 661384f6 took 445 s and this branch 562 s, both exit 0. So the suite was already over the ceiling before this branch's new sections (row 5b-233's lookup, row 5b-274's activity list) were added; they add about a quarter. `SUITE_TIMEOUT` in [`tools/check-gaps.py`](../../tools/check-gaps.py) says 300 s is about four times the slowest suite measured there, 78 s on 2026-09-10. NOT measured on an unloaded machine or on the owner's PC. | **OPEN - recorded, not fixed.** Either the suite is made faster or `check-gaps` gives it a ceiling of its own, and the choice is for whoever owns that rule; raising the one ceiling for every suite would let a hung suite wait longer before it is named. |
| **403** | **THE COMPANION'S PAGE ACTIONS THAT REACH REVIT STILL GUESS HOW A CALL ENDED, OR WRITE REFUSED FOR A REPLY THAT IS NOT OK.** Carried from [row 5b-389](section-5b-rows-376-400.md) on 2026-10-09, when that row's `revit_*` half was fixed. Four page actions in mcp/server/heron_mcp_server.py reach Revit and write their own line on the activity list. `_apply_change` (companion_apply) records revit_change's answer with no outcome, so that line is guessed from its first line. It calls `_through(revit_change, origin="companion")` with no reply_out, a line tests/test_companion.py pins as text. `_apply_table` (companion_table_apply) writes `refused` only when the fragment's resultJson says nothing was applied, and no outcome otherwise, so a lost answer or revit_busy is guessed. `_sprinkler_layout_place` (companion_sprinkler_layout_place) writes `refused` for any revit_change reply that is not ok, so a lost placement answer shows as refused. Its good line is guessed, and its earlier refusals write no line. `_load_settings` (companion_load, the page's Load from Revit) writes `refused` for any revit_read reply that is not ok, so a lost answer or revit_busy shows as refused. Its good line is guessed. Its earlier returns (NotBound, list_views not answered, the pin's refusal, no active view) write no line at all. Since row 5b-389, `_through` hands the body's own label back in reply_out["ended"], and that is the hook the repair can use. Read in the source, NOT RUN. | **OPEN - recorded, not fixed.** The repair records the label `_through` hands back in reply_out["ended"] on each page action's line. `_apply_change` needs a reply_out on its `_through` call, which changes the line tests/test_companion.py pins, so the Companion's owner should agree to that first. |
| **404** | **THE LOOKUP'S FIRST OPEN REBUILDS AN EMPTY SHARED STORE FROM WHICHEVER CHECKOUT ASKS, AND `heron_scope.py --rebuild` REBUILDS IT FROM ANY CHECKOUT.** Found 2026-10-09 while closing [row 5b-233](section-5b-rows-226-250.md)'s leftovers. Read in the source, not run. In `mcp/server/heron_brain.py`, `_Open._prepare` calls `heron_scope.rebuild()` whenever the GLOBAL store is empty. `rebuild()` builds from the asking checkout's own cards, and `_prepare` never asks `heron_scope.rebuild_refusal()`. So a new or deleted shared store, first asked from a worktree or from the main folder on a feature branch, is built from that branch's cards. `refresh()` never removes the rows that adds. `python brain/heron_scope.py --rebuild` has no check either. The heron-ship skill tells a person to run it from the main checkout on main, but nothing stops a run from anywhere else. Both are row 5b-233's class: one store every chat on the PC reads, holding whichever checkout last built it. `tools/check-routing.py`'s `store_for_this_tree()`, the tools that call it, and the lookup's warm-up and rebuild advice now refuse it. These two paths do not. | **OPEN - recorded, not repaired.** Someone has to decide the fix: refuse on the shared store, or build it from the main checkout's cards, the ones `refreshes_from()` names. NOT RUN: neither path was run here. |
| **405** | **`COMBINE_FAMILY_FORMS` MEASURED ONLY WHAT REVIT KEPT IN THE COMBINATION - A FORM REVIT LEFT OUT WENT UNSEEN, SO A COIL CAME BACK HALF GONE AS A GOOD CUT, AND A CUT REVIT COULD NOT MAKE CAME BACK AS "NO SOLID IN IT TO MEASURE".** Seen twice on 2026-10-07 in the owner's session (Revit 2024, session 9240): Family1, 24 solid 15-degree revolves of a stacked coil spring plus two end voids, and Family11, a seat extrusion on Center (Front/Back) plus a void on Ref. Level rounding its corners - both threw *"Revit made a combination with no solid in it to measure. NOTHING from this call was kept."* while 2 solids + 4 voids and 1 solid + 5 voids worked in the same session. **Reproduced on a scratch family, never on Family1 or Family11** - `CombineScratch.rfa`, Metric Generic Model, `%TEMP%\heron-work\combine`, every combine rolled back. **The cause, measured:** when Revit's join or cut fails on a form, it leaves that form's material out of the combination and still lists it as a member - after `Regenerate` every member reads 0 L of its own and the combination holds only the rest - and says *"Can't keep elements joined"* only when the transaction commits, after the fragment has measured. A coil of 24 revolves, six circles "circle 60,Z 8" a 24 mm pitch apart, each revolve 1 mm higher than the last, with voids "rect -90,-90 90,90" at Z -20..3.5 and 152.5..176: 26 of 26 listed, ONE solid of 0.2251 L of the 0.4548 L named, every other revolve gone - and the old code reported *"0.455 L before, 0.225 L after - 0.23 L taken away"* and passed; read back after the commit, the combination still held 0.2251 L. A second coil whose revolves overlap by a degree each way had 12 of its 24 left out too. The seat as described cut correctly (27.461 to 27.091 L); with the void's corner arcs typed to whole millimetres ("arc 537,-162 560,-105", its sides still on the seat's faces) Revit left the seat out entirely and main's code threw the owner's exact words; the same void with its sides a millimetre past the faces cut (27.461 to 27.113 L). Not the causes first suspected: no `GeometryInstance` (one Solid, any detail level, non-visible objects included), and two solids apart or touching, and a void that misses, all behave. Also measured: a void's own geometry is an inside-out solid of NEGATIVE volume, Revit's inside/outside answer for one reads backwards, and `BooleanOperationsUtils` cannot stand in as a check - it failed on the coil and ADDED a void's volume to a box. | **FIXED, PROVEN 2026-10-08 - signed by Ajmal PS.** Points inside each solid are taken before combining (one per face, from the chord along its normal); the combination is made first in a sub-transaction, always rolled back, and every point no void covers must lie inside the result - judged by counting face crossings along three skewed rays, which holds for an inside-out void too. A solid left out refuses the call with nothing kept, naming each form and Revit's own words; only then is the real combination made, and read form by form again. "Void took nothing away" and "join cannot add material" unchanged. Measured on `CombineScratch`, rolled back: the coil (both kinds) refused naming the 12 revolves, and a KEPT refusal left 0 combinations; the whole-millimetre seat refused naming the seat; seat 0.371 L, seat 1 mm past 0.348 L, box + hole 0.785 L, box + void that misses still refused, two boxes apart or touching 32 L. Compiles 2020 to 2027. Proof recorded in its card, fingerprint b29898ba6396b9b9: the seat cut 0.371 L and read back; the coil refused naming the 12 revolves, nothing combined. **Also seen, not fixed:** two solids 600 mm apart join with the words "they only touched". **Written as 5b-370, renumbered 405 at the merge:** #444 merged its own 5b-370 first. The fragment's two code comments still say 5b-370 - they are inside the proof seal, so they are corrected at its next re-proof, as 5b-69 set out; the card and its cases say 5b-405. |
| **406** | **A CARD MERGED INTO MAIN NEVER REACHED THE SHARED KNOWLEDGE STORE UNTIL SOMEBODY RAN `python brain/heron_scope.py --rebuild` BY HAND - SO ON THE OWNER'S PC *"list the materials in this family"* COULD NOT REACH `LIST_MATERIALS` AND WENT TO `SET_FAMILY_FORM_MATERIAL`, A CHANGE.** Found 2026-10-10 on the owner's PC: `%APPDATA%\Heron\knowledge\global.db` held 480 fragment rows beside 481 cards in `brain/fragments/`, and the missing one was `list-materials` (FRG-ELE-077, merged in #450 as `36f02b54`). `heron_brain._Open._prepare` calls `heron_scope.rebuild()` only for an EMPTY store and otherwise `refresh()`, which by design never added a row or removed one ([row 249](#)'s *Not done here*; [NEEDS-CHECKING BD3](../needs-checking/group-bd.md)) - and it had already looked at the new card and MARKED it, so every later lookup skipped it without opening it. The owner is a modeller, and a command to remember after every update is a step that does not happen. **Reproduced 2026-10-10 on a scratch shared store** - `APPDATA` pointed at a work folder outside the repository, the real store untouched - built by the code as it stood from main `36f02b54`, FRG-ELE-077's row taken out and its card marked by one refresh, exactly the owner's state. On the trained backend with the cross-encoder re-ranker, *"list the materials in this family"* answered `SET_FAMILY_FORM_MATERIAL` and *"show me all the materials in this project"* `REPORT_PIPE_SEGMENTS`, both by the hybrid route, and the store stayed at 480. | **FIXED 2026-10-10 - in a chat started after this merges.** `refresh()` also asks a new `_library_moves()`. In the SHARED store only, read from a main checkout on branch `main` or from an installed Heron: a card folder no row names is parsed whatever its mark says, and ADDED when it validates; a row whose card folder has GONE from main is DROPPED when that row was made from main's card - its folder is in `rows_from`. Same single short `BEGIN IMMEDIATE`, everything slow outside it, and every condition asked again under the lock. **Never:** in a private store - a suite's, CI's, a measurement's; from a worktree ([row 131](#)); from the main folder on a feature branch, whose new cards are unmerged ([row 233](#)'s hazard); a row a session planted for its unmerged card, which was never in `rows_from`; a card whose `fragment.yaml` is missing while its folder is still there, which is git writing it anew; anything at all when main's library holds no card. A NEW card that does not validate is not added, and the lookup carries on; in the shared store it is parsed again on each lookup until it validates, because the file that finishes it may not be `fragment.yaml`. The capability map, the search index and the vectors all read the rows and run after the refresh inside the same `_Open`, so a card added there is found in that same lookup; a dropped row's vector stays in `vectors` - `heron_embed.index` never prunes a fragment's - but every route keeps only the ids the store holds (`heron_retrieve`'s `keep`), as after any rebuild. **The same probe after:** both sentences answer `LIST_MATERIALS` by the **identity** route on the first lookup, the store is at 481, and *"select all ducts"* is unchanged (identity, `FILTER_ELEMENTS_BY_CATEGORY`). A quiet lookup's refresh, 20 runs over the 481 cards: median 3.9 ms after against 4.4 ms before. [`tests/test_scope_store.py`](../../tests/test_scope_store.py) section 5 gains `following_main()` and one private-store check: ten of the new checks fail on the code before, and each guard above, removed alone, turns at least one red (2026-10-10). **Not done here:** a card whose id changed inside its own folder still has a row by folder, so nothing is added and `--rebuild` is still the answer; rows a worktree's `rebuild()` put into the shared store for cards main also holds are not rewritten back ([row 233](#)) - only the cards it added are dropped and the ones it left out added; and a chat started before this merges runs the old code until the Claude app is restarted. #449, merged the same day, added `drift()` - a REPORT of the same folder-name comparison against the asking checkout's cards - and moved `on_main_branch()` into `heron_scope`; this uses that one and keeps `refresh()`'s `walked`, so `drift()` still reads the walk it took. |
| **407** | **`revit_change` HANDS BACK EVERY VARIABLE A WRITE FRAGMENT'S SCRIPT LEFT, NOT THE `provides` ITS CARD DECLARES, AND ENDS EVERY REPLY WITH THE SAME *"was KEPT"* PARAGRAPH - SO ONE AFTERNOON'S TWO FAMILY BUILDS CARRIED 322,725 CHARACTERS, ABOUT 150k TOKENS, BACK INTO THE CHAT, AND ABOUT 70% OF IT SAID NOTHING NEW.** Measured 2026-10-09 building a fire alarm control panel and a duty/standby pump set on the owner's new laptop ([row 5b-378](section-5b-rows-376-400.md) has the session), and re-derived 2026-10-10 from that chat's transcript. **172 `revit_change` replies, 322,725 characters as sent, 314,993 once the JSON is decoded:** 42.0% of the decoded text is lines naming a variable the fragment's card does not declare under `contract.provides` - ``along Func`3``, ``axisOf Func`2``, `invariant CultureInfo`, ``plane Tuple`4``, `inPlane 3 item(s) [System.Int32[], ...]`, `datums 3 item(s)` with each tuple printed under the Revit API's own type name for a reference plane or a level, `halfMillimetre 0.00164041994750656` - and 2.8% more is the fragment's own needs echoed back, about 45% together; 24.5% is the *"'NAME' was KEPT: Revit accepted the transaction rather than rolling it back ..."* paragraph, word for word in 170 of the 172. 127 of the 172 calls were `CREATE_FAMILY_EXTRUSION`, which alone gave 116,590 of the 132,308 undeclared characters; `SET_FAMILY_FORM_MATERIAL`, `SET_FAMILY_TYPE_VALUES`, `COMBINE_FAMILY_FORMS`, `CREATE_FAMILY_REVOLUTION` and `SET_FAMILY_FORM_VISIBILITY` did the same. About 2.2 characters a token, measured on the 12 replies that came back on their own: the context grew 6,968 tokens for their 15,260 characters. **Why, read:** `Report` in [`RevitFragment.cs`](../../revit/Heron.Revit.Addin/RevitFragment.cs) puts every name in `state.Variables` into `provides` except what the host bound and is still the host's (`StillTheHosts`) - the card's declared `provides` is never consulted - and `_provides_lines` in [`heron_mcp_server.py`](../../mcp/server/heron_mcp_server.py) prints each one as an aligned line. **Already recorded, and narrower:** [row 304](section-5b-rows-301-325.md) names private helpers returned as results by `REPORT_FAMILY_TEMPLATE` alone, and [a 2026-09-22 note](a-drawing-set-and-a-house-plan-2026-09-22.md) saw ``readValue Func`2`` from `read-element-parameters`; neither counts what it costs a chat, and neither covers the trailer. **For whoever fixes it:** the declared `provides` is a filter both doors already hold; whether the trailer comes once per batch rather than once per call is a wording decision as well as a size one, because it carries the undo rule (Article 8). **Not checked:** whether a later step reads an undeclared name - `StillTheHosts` serves both the answer and the chain, so the filter belongs on the reply until that is known; whether a proof record reads one; the share on a project-model job rather than a family build. | **FIXED 2026-10-11 by [#472](https://github.com/Ajmalpshaik/Heron-AI/pull/472), in the MCP server.** `_provides_lines` names a value that holds code - a delegate, a reflection or culture object, a namespace string, a list whose items print as System or vendor types - on one `NOT PRINTED` line instead of printing it. A value the card does not declare still prints when it holds data: 317 fragments' proofs show undeclared values that are real results, so printing only the declared ones was measured and rejected. Measured on the 177 replies of the 2026-10-09 chat: the results block 205,275 to 161,531 characters (-21%). The add-in's KEPT verdict paragraph is the add-in's to write and is unchanged. Held by `tests/test_read_door.py` section 7. NOT RUN in a live chat since the merge. **AND 2026-10-17, THE REST OF IT - a short change report, the detail on disk; compiled for all eight releases, NOT YET RUN IN REVIT.** The add-in now counts what Revit says a write changed - `DocumentChanged`, through `RevitChangedSet`, the collector the Keep-or-Discard box of D-126 already uses, extended rather than a second beside it, collected from `group.Start()` to just before the modeller is asked and Assimilate or the rollback, only `TransactionCommitted` on the target, a setup step's transactions (`label - stepName`) apart from the fragment's (`label`), each element's `Category.Name` read once (`HeronChangeReport`, proved in `tests/Heron.ChangeReport.TestHost`) - and the dispatcher keeps every element and the whole reply in `HeronPaths.Runs\<workflow id>.json`, DERIVED and pruned by `runs.retainDays`, with `elementsAdded`, `elementsModified`, `elementsDeleted` and `changeEvents` on the audit line (Article 22). `revit_change` prints two change lines and a `record:` line, the KEPT paragraph once per batch and the add-in's one sentence - the undo rule whole - on the rest, and counts rather than prints a value the last reply of the same capability showed word for word; `heron_run_record` (READ) pages a record back. **Replayed** - 172 kept changes in 39 batches shaped from `CREATE_FAMILY_EXTRUSION`'s code, through the real `revit_change` and an MCP SDK - 213,332 characters on `main` (ec400a37, re-measured after D-126) and 160,364 on this change, 24.8% less; an add-in from before the change prints byte for byte as it did. **For the owner, two wording decisions taken and his to reverse:** the KEPT paragraph is printed once per batch, a batch being calls less than 3 s apart (`heron_run_records.BATCH_GAP_S`), with the undo rule on every reply; and #472's "declared only" stays rejected - a value is left out only when the last reply showed it word for word and a run record holds it. The DRAFT line that used to follow every change is gone on its own account: since D-117 a capability that is not PROVEN is refused before anything is sent. Whether Revit raises the event where this needs it is [NEEDS-CHECKING Group EV](../needs-checking/group-ev.md). |
| **408** | **`.claude/skills/heron-session/bin/hook_log.py` LINE 39 HOLDS `\H` IN AN ORDINARY DOCSTRING - `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Heron\logs` - SO UNDER PYTHON 3.12 A HOOK THAT IMPORTS IT PRINTS *"SyntaxWarning: invalid escape sequence '\H'"* TO ITS STDERR.** The same class as [row 379](section-5b-rows-376-400.md), one file over. Seen 2026-10-09 in the session [row 5b-378](section-5b-rows-376-400.md) measured: two hook results in that chat's transcript carry it - the SessionStart hook's stderr, 204 bytes, and the PreToolUse hook's on the chat's only Write, 226 bytes. Reproduced 2026-10-10 on the owner's PC, Python 3.12.10, by compiling the file with warnings shown: `hook_log.py:39: SyntaxWarning: invalid escape sequence '\H'`. `heron_guard.py`, `main_moved.py` and `session_line.py` each import it to write their diary line. **It repeats because no Python 3.12 cache of the file is kept:** the main checkout's `bin/__pycache__` holds only `hook_log.cpython-311.pyc`, so every 3.12 run compiles it again - why 3.12 writes none there was not checked. **Cosmetic today:** the diary line is still written and no hook's decision changes. A later Python is due to make the sequence an error, and `import hook_log` sits inside guards written never to raise (`heron_guard.diary` catches `Exception`), so the hooks' diary would then stop without a word. The owner asked why it went one by one. | **RUN 2026-10-17 on Revit 2020 - three `PASS`es on the scratch "Project1", every leg rolled back; SIGNED by Ajmal PS and PROVEN the same day (NEEDS-CHECKING Group BA, BA10 and BA11).** Several: `confirmCount=3` issued Seq. 1-3 in one call, `confirmCount=2` refused naming all three and both numbers. One call is one undo entry and all or nothing. The writes go un-issue (last sequence first), text columns, numbering (last first), show, issue (first sequence first) - the orders Revit took on the job - and the renumber check judges each revision by the state the set will be in when numbering is written, so the request refused for Seq. 1 goes through in one call. A lock or renumber refusal on any revision changes none of them. That measurement also showed, on main and unchanged here, *"who issued revision 3"* and *"what is the date of revision 2"* reaching EDIT_REVISION, *"show me the revision schedule"* CREATE_REVISION and *"what revisions are on this sheet"* SET_SHEET_REVISIONS - [5b-246](section-5b-rows-226-250.md)'s shape, recorded here, not fixed; EDIT_REVISION cannot change the model on a question, since every value empty is refused as *"nothing was asked for"*. An independent read of the code before push found a refusal for an unreadable `confirmCount` that named none of the revisions, and a read-back that throws after Revit took a value reported as refused (from version 2) - both fixed, the read-back now ends the call so the add-in rolls it back. | **410** | **`ADD_FAMILY_CONNECTOR` FINDS NO FACE ON A FORM THAT A VOID HAS CUT, SO A FLANGED VALVE'S CONNECTOR COULD NOT GO ON ITS FLANGE.** Found 2026-10-10 building a fixed-size 4 inch OS&Y gate valve in Family1 (Revit 2020, session 36948, a new unsaved family). Every flanged or bored MEP family built this way meets it. **Fix when owned:** also search the faces of any combination the forms are in, keeping the exactly-one-face rule. | **FIXED IN CODE, DRAFT - not yet run in Revit (2026-10-11, PR #474).** [`ADD_FAMILY_CONNECTOR`](../../brain/fragments/add-family-connector/fragment.yaml) version 3 also reads every combination's geometry, as `COMBINE_FAMILY_FORMS` reads one, when no form's own face lies on the plane - exactly one, or refused with the count. | **411** | **`batch-prove` CANNOT JUDGE A FRAGMENT WHOSE RESULT IS A SENTENCE: `EDIT_REVISION` VERSION 2 CAME BACK `POSITIVE UNREADABLE` WITH BOTH LEGS HELD.** Found 2026-10-10 re-proving `edit-revision` on Project2 (a scratch project, Revit 2020, session 36948) with [the job file](../../tools/jobs/issue-revisions-revit-2020-2026-10-10.yaml). `changed` is declared a string result - version 2 made it one joined string so the reply prints all of it. The positive returned `changed` *issued by (blank) -> 'M.Sagheer'; issued to (blank) -> 'A.Rahmani'; issued*; the negative returned `changed` empty and `alreadyThat` naming the description and the date - a real contrast, read by hand. The runner still said *"POSITIVE UNREADABLE ... cannot be read as a quantity"*, because it judges a result by a count moving off zero. `heron_validate.py draft` drafted the proof all the same. **Fix when owned:** read a non-empty string result as found and an empty one as nothing, so every fragment that returns a sentence can be batch-proved. | **FIXED 2026-10-11.** A provide declared `type: string` **and** `role: result` is read as text: found when it says something, nothing when it is empty, blank or `(null)` - `string_results` and `_as_found` in [`brain/heron_validate.py`](../../brain/heron_validate.py), used by `positive_worked`, `looks_empty`, `draft`, `accept` and `batch-prove`'s `judge`. A string declared `role: accounting`, or with no role, is read as before, and `expect:` still decides what is judged. **Both legs, never one:** a one-word answer such as `SupplyAir` has the shape of a bare type name, which the negative skipped as a helper object, so reading it only in the positive would have PASSED the same word in both legs. **Beside a count or a flag, the count decides (D-51)** - found by review before merging: `set-material-colour` declares `changed: int` beside `wasColour` and `nowColour`, which read back the colour whether or not anything changed, so asked for the colour a material already has it answers `changed 0` with both filled, and the sentence alone would have PASSED it; the old signing gate's `_result_appeared` rescue signed exactly that shape. Now it is `POSITIVE EMPTY` in both, and `expect:` naming the sentence alone still judges it. [`tests/test_batch_prove.py`](../../tests/test_batch_prove.py) gained two sections and [`tests/test_validate_agent.py`](../../tests/test_validate_agent.py) one, for `accept` and `draft`; fourteen of their checks were seen to FAIL on the judge as it stood, none crashed, and breaking each call site in turn turns its own check red. **This row's own run record, re-judged:** `POSITIVE UNREADABLE` before, `PASS - positive: changed issued by (blank) -> 'M.Sagheer'; ...` after - and `batch-prove` now agrees with `accept`, which had let it through on `_result_appeared`'s comparison. **Every run record on the owner's laptop re-judged by both trees (310, measured 2026-10-11): five verdicts moved.** `edit-revision`, `create-mep-system-type` and `duplicate-view-template` went UNREADABLE to PASS (the last two already PROVEN through that same comparison). Two went stricter, and both are **seen, not fixed**: `report-family-parameters` went PASS to NEG NOT EMPTY - its negative returned `currentTypeName Standard`, the same word as its positive, which its contract declares `role: result`, and it passed before only because the word has no space in it; `report-findings` went UNREADABLE to NEG NOT EMPTY, its `report` writing *"NOTHING WAS CHECKED ..."* in the negative. Each is a contract calling context, or a sentence that always speaks, a result - owed a read of the contract (`role: accounting`, or proof by tracking), not changed here. **Also seen, not fixed:** in `draft_from_record`, a `provides()` that throws leaves `declared_names` unassigned, so the line after the `except` would raise `NameError`. **Not run in Revit:** judging needs none, and `edit-revision` is PROVEN, so `batch-prove` answers `ALREADY` for it now. |

| **421** | **NO TOOL TURNED THE DATES TYPED ON SHEETS INTO REVISIONS, SO ON 2026-10-10 ONE REQUEST TOOK ABOUT THIRTY CALLS, EACH ITS OWN UNDO ENTRY - AND A REVISION NAME TYPED FROM AN EARLIER READ WENT STALE PART WAY.** Found 2026-10-10 on *4355-BHVD-3D-50C10-BL001A* (Revit 2020, 19 sheets), reported in the owner's brief of 2026-10-11. `LIST_DATES_ON_SHEETS` listed 7 dates on 15 sheets, and the modeller asked for one revision per date, description *IFI - Issued for Information*, on the sheets that carry each date. N - description*, typed from an earlier read; when the owner deleted Seq. 1, Revit renumbered the rest and a name typed minutes before (Seq. 8) named nothing. A chain could not have done it: it cannot loop over dates, and every name it carries goes stale the same way. `EDIT_REVISION`'s half of the same job - issuing the seven one at a time - is row 5b-409 of [PR #469](https://github.com/Ajmalpshaik/Heron-AI/pull/469), not repeated here. | **FIXED at DRAFT 2026-10-11, NOT YET RUN IN REVIT.** New `CREATE_REVISIONS_FROM_SHEET_DATES` (FRG-SHT-029, MODIFY): one call, one Undo entry. Refused with nothing changed when no sheet carries a date, the description is empty, or two revisions already stand for one date. Compiled for 2020 to 2027. NEEDS REAL REVIT.** The test, on a scratch copy: wall 1 of a type whose core is material A, nothing painted; wall 2 of another type, with A painted on one face; ask for A over Walls with includePaint false, then true. The card is right if the answers are {1} then {1, 2}; the comments are right if they are {1} then {2}. If the comments are right, the repair is `FIND_UNUSED_MATERIALS`' - read both sets and union them when paint is asked for, at `:103` and `:237` - and the negative case should also expect the second answer to hold every element of the first. |

So *the rooms are missing* now reaches `CHECK_ROOM_MEP_COMPLETENESS` on lexical as it already did on `model` on `main`: a write to a read, against that card's own table, recorded rather than tuned. `check-routing` on `main` reads only *-> here* rows as claims, so it is on neither list. **Measured before and after on 138 sentences**, on both backends - 93 written before any card was touched (the three; 12 other ways to list, show or select rooms or spaces; 7 other ways to ask what rooms lack; 39 room jobs and edges, `PLACE_ROOMS`' own first; 32 counts and lists from other domains) and 49 written after the first measurement to see whether its two lexical moves were a pattern (*show me all the ...* and *list the ...* for other categories, and *which ... are missing ...*), 4 in both: answers that write (MODIFY or above) **on `36f02b54` 47 to 39 on lexical and 48 to 42 on `model`** (on `661384f6` 47 to 40 and 48 to 42), **and no sentence's answer became riskier on either backend at either commit.** Moved on lexical, besides the three: *show me all the doors* off `PLACE_HOSTED_FAMILY` on `36f02b54` (off `ZOOM_TO_ELEMENTS` on `661384f6`); *rooms missing services*, *the rooms are missing* to the check; *how many spaces are there in the model* and *which rooms are missing from the schedule* off `PLACE_ROOMS` to the filter and to `READ_SCHEDULE_CONTENTS`; *how many rooms are there* (which that change declares), *how many doors are there*, *count the doors on level 1* and *show me all the pipes* to the filter, read to read; *show me all the dampers* from `ZOOM_TO_ELEMENTS` to `LIST_REVISIONS`, a read that is wrong in meaning. On `model`: *what services are missing from the rooms* and *rooms missing services* off `PLACE_ROOMS` to the check, *select all the rooms* off it to `SELECT_UNENCLOSED_ROOMS` - a read, though the filter is the right one - and *count the doors on level 1* to the filter, read to read. **With that change** (#463's head `30624b1d` on `36f02b54`, merged locally with both sides kept) against it alone: writes 46 to 39 on lexical and 47 to 41 on `model`, no answer riskier, and one write moved to another on `model` - *list the rooms on level 1*, `PLACE_ROOMS` to `SET_ROOM_LIMITS`. Together the two help each other: *how many rooms are there* and *show me all the rooms* then reach the filter on ranking alone as well. **Still reaching a change after this:** of the 12 other listings, 9 on lexical and 10 on `model` - *list all the rooms*, *show me the rooms*, *what rooms are in this model*, *what rooms are there*, *show all the rooms on this level*, *list the spaces* and *list all the MEP spaces* reach `PLACE_ROOMS` on both; *list the rooms on level 1* `SET_ROOM_LIMITS` on lexical and `PLACE_ROOMS` on `model`; *list every room in the model* and *show me all the spaces* `PLACE_ROOMS` on `model`; *select all the rooms* `SET_ROOM_LIMITS` on lexical. Of the 7 other ways to ask what rooms lack, 1 on each: *which spaces are missing services* (`PLACE_ROOMS`); and of the later set, *which rooms are missing supply air*, *which spaces are missing a diffuser* and *rooms are missing from this level* reach `PLACE_ROOMS` on both, *which rooms are missing their tags* `TAG_ROOMS`. The kind stays open for row 355's reason: `PLACE_ROOMS`' card sits nearest any question about rooms or spaces, and the fix is a re-design of it measured on both backends, or reopening row 109's "warn, don't re-rank" - the owner's call, not a wording tweak. **Seen while measuring, not this row's to fix** - the same shape outside rooms, all on `main` before this change: *show me all the walls* (`SET_WALL_CONSTRAINTS`), *columns* (`ARRANGE_TAGS_TO_VIEW_EDGES`), *views* (`SET_VIEW_UNDERLAY`) and *ceilings* (`CREATE_FROM_ROOM_BOUNDARIES`) on lexical; *show me all the floors* (`CREATE_LEVEL`), *furniture* (`SET_CATEGORY_SOLID_FILL`), *equipment* (`SET_VIEW_WORKSET_VISIBILITY`) and *tags* (`TAG_ROOMS`) on `model`; *show me all the air terminals* (`SET_FAMILY_CATEGORY`, lexical; `SET_AIR_TERMINAL_FLOW`, `model`); *list all the walls* (`SET_WALL_CONSTRAINTS`), *list the pipes* and *show all the pipes* (`CAP_OPEN_PIPE_ENDS`) and *list the windows* (`CREATE_SHEET_LIST`) on both, and *show me all the pipes* (`CAP_OPEN_PIPE_ENDS`) on `model`; *list the doors* and *list the views* (`CREATE_SHEET_LIST`), *list the families* (`EXPORT_FAMILIES`, PUBLISH) on lexical; *which ducts are missing insulation* and *which pipes are missing insulation* (`SET_MEP_INSULATION`) on both. **Gates, before and after on `36f02b54`, both backends:** `check-routing` - the same exit, no question answered by a write, the claimed-and-not-reached lists unchanged (21 lexical, 19 `model`), every new *-> NAME* row a declared capability, and four new contests on ranking alone: the three new sentences on the filter (*list the rooms* #15 behind `PLACE_ROOMS`, *show me all the rooms* #2 behind `READ_SPACE_LOADS`, *show me all the doors* #2 behind `SELECT_BY_HOST`), and *what is missing*, which `DESCRIBE_BLANK_PARAMETERS` holds by identity and `CHECK_ROOM_MEP_COMPLETENESS` now wins by ranking - with rank ripples of two to five (on `661384f6` also *show me the links*, `LIST_LINKED_MODELS`' by identity, the filter's by ranking); `check-risk-crossings` and `score-routing` (the owner's questions) - the same answer question for question; `check-skill-routing` - the same answers but one, on `model`: `find-blank-parameters`' *what is missing before I issue this* goes from `READ_SCHEDULE_CONTENTS` to `CHECK_ROOM_MEP_COMPLETENESS`, a miss to a miss, read to read; `check-intrusion` - the same exit; on `model` `PLACE_ROOMS` is in 70 shortlists where it was 67, `PLACE_ROOM_AT_POINT` and `TAG_ROOMS` 69 where 66 and 67, and the twelfth place is `REPORT_ROOM_SPACE_DATA` at 55 where it was `LABEL_FAMILY_RADIUS` at 53; on lexical `CENTER_ROOM_TAGS` 81 where 78, and the twelfth `PLACE_ROOMS` at 50 where it was `DIMENSION_ROOMS` at 50 - the four new sentences' own shortlists. The live `heron_lookup` reads the main checkout's cards, so its answer changes once this is merged and that checkout is brought level. **Re-measured 2026-10-11 on `main` `6b3bd57d`** (28 commits on, PR #463 merged), every run on scratch stores in clean venvs - lexical with no model, trained without the re-ranker the live laptop now runs. Two things had moved. *show me all the doors* reaches `SELECT_BY_HOST`, a read, on `main` on both backends, and declared on the filter it sent `PLACE_HOSTED_FAMILY`'s claim *put doors in the walls at these points* (row 319's, which `tests/test_routing_phrases.py` asks back through the search) to `CREATE_WALL` on lexical - so it is withdrawn. With that: answers that write over the 138 sentences 46 to 39 on lexical and 46 to 40 on the trained backend, none riskier on either, and one write moved to another on the trained backend - *list the rooms on level 1*, `PLACE_ROOMS` to `SET_ROOM_LIMITS`. `check-routing` - the same exit on both, the claimed-and-not-reached lists unchanged (30 lexical, 33 trained), and two new contests on ranking alone for sentences other cards hold by identity, *count these elements by room* (`COUNT_BY_SPATIAL_CONTAINER`) and *what is missing* (`DESCRIBE_BLANK_PARAMETERS`), both now `CHECK_ROOM_MEP_COMPLETENESS` by ranking, while row 401's *how many rooms are there* leaves the contested list, the filter now winning it by ranking too; `check-risk-crossings` and `score-routing` - identical, question for question; `check-skill-routing` - the same one move as before, a miss to a miss; `check-intrusion` - the same exit. |
| **427** | **`_companion_module` PUTS THE COMPANION'S FOLDER ON PYTHON'S IMPORT PATH AGAIN ON EVERY CALL, SO A CHAT'S HERON SERVER ADDS ONE OR TWO COPIES OF THE SAME ENTRY FOR EVERY TOOL CALL IT ANSWERS.** Found 2026-10-11 measuring why a 165-form generator family took 24 minutes on the owner's laptop (chat `b94ed61c`, Revit 2024; [docs/04 §8.7](../04-heron-mcp.md)), and re-read on `main` at `11a0805b`. [`mcp/server/heron_mcp_server.py:4527`](../../mcp/server/heron_mcp_server.py) runs `sys.path.insert(0, <...>/companion)` with no check that the folder is already there. It runs once for every outer tool call, from `_note` (line 365), which writes the Companion's activity line for `_recorded`; and once more for every change from the chat that Revit kept, from `_offer_change` (line 1894) - which then returns at once, because `SHOW_CHANGES` is `False` (`mcp/companion/heron_companion.py:407`). So that build's 195 kept writes and 3 reads added about 393 copies of one folder - derived from the code, not read out of the live process. The file's two other inserts, lines 1303-1304 and 3540-3541, check `if brain_path not in sys.path` first; this one does not. Reproduced in a scratch Python: the same statement three times leaves three copies. **No cost was measured**: a module already imported is found in `sys.modules` before the path is searched, and the server's whole time per call outside Revit was a median 24 ms. **Not checked:** how many entries a long-lived server reaches, and whether an import made for the first time late in its life is slowed by walking them. | **OPEN - recorded, not repaired.** The repair is the guard the other two inserts already carry. |
| **428** | **EVERY `revit_change` AND `revit_read` THAT NAMES ITS CAPABILITY OPENS EVERY CARD'S `fragment.yaml`, IN NAME ORDER, UNTIL ONE DECLARES THAT CAPABILITY - NOTHING IS KEPT BETWEEN CALLS, AND THE WALK TAKES 8 TO 37 MS A CALL.** Found 2026-10-11 in the same measurement as [row 427](#). [`_fragment_for`, `mcp/server/heron_mcp_server.py:1007`](../../mcp/server/heron_mcp_server.py) lists `brain/fragments`, opens each folder's `fragment.yaml` and reads it to the last line - it does not stop at the `capability:` line - and returns on the first card that matches. `revit_change` calls it at line 1552, `revit_read` at 2623, `_contract_lines` at 3525, and three more places. **Measured on the owner's laptop** by timing a scratch copy of the step, with no store, pipe or Revit touched: 7.6 to 9.6 ms for a `create-family-*` card, 36 to 37 ms for a `set-family-*` card, which sorts later and so is reached after more files. Derived from those: about 2.5 s across the build's 198 calls. Heron's own time outside Revit on those calls was a median 24 ms by the MCP client's log joined to the Revit audit (38 to 75 ms from the transcript, which adds Claude Code's own handling), so this one walk is a large share of Heron's part, and a very small share of a 1,423 s build whose model turns were 95% of it. **The docstring chose this on purpose** - *"speed is not the thing to optimise here"* - because a provider and a status are facts about a file and the retrieval index was stale once. Recorded as both: the stated choice, and the measured cost. **Not checked:** how the time grows with the library, and the office PC. | **OPEN - recorded, not repaired.** Whether to keep anything between calls - something that still answers from the files, keyed on each card's modified time, say - goes against a reason the docstring gives, so it is the server owner's choice, not a repair in passing. |

The real run sends `validate` without `--allow-publish`, and `mcp/client/heron_bridge_client.py` refuses that for these two risks before anything is sent - [D-106](../decisions/D-106.md) point 6: no batch sends an ADMIN or PUBLISH fragment. `job_refusal` checked the name, the status, the setup names, the negative and the `expect:` list, and never the fragment's risk, so the one check that decides whether the client will send it at all was left to the run. The same held for `tools/jobs/family-settings-shared-2026-10-01.yaml` and `tools/jobs/replace-family-shared-parameters-2026-10-12.yaml` once that card is ADMIN. **The same shape, one step back:** `validate` also refuses a SETUP step whose risk is above MODIFY or unreadable, whatever the flags (a step is an arrangement, and arranging a proof never needs to export, save or set up the project), and `job_refusal` checked only that a setup step was a fragment - so a job with such a step read WOULD RUN and came back DID NOT RUN. | **FIXED 2026-10-12, in `job_refusal`, with the client's own readers** - `risk_refusal` and `fragment_risk` from `heron_bridge_client.py`, imported and not copied, reading the card where the library found it. A PUBLISH or ADMIN fragment is now its own bucket, **WILL NOT RUN IN A BATCH**, in the dry run AND the real run, naming the risk, the switch and the one command that does send it (`heron_bridge_client.py validate --allow-publish --write ...` for that fragment by name); nothing is handed to `validate`, and the exit code stays 0 - the job file is not broken, a batch is the wrong way to send it. An unreadable or unknown risk is REFUSED with the client's own sentence. `--allow-publish` is never added by the batch. A setup step above MODIFY, or with a risk nobody can read, is REFUSED naming the step and its risk - checked BEFORE the fragment's own risk, because the by-name command fails on that step too, and there is no command that sends it. Evidence: `tests/test_batch_prove.py` `test_an_admin_or_publish_fragment_is_never_sent_by_a_batch`, with ADMIN, PUBLISH, MODIFY and unknown-risk fixture cards - 12 checks red against the code before the fix, all green after; the setup-step checks (an ADMIN and a PUBLISH step behind a MODIFY and an ADMIN fragment, a step with no card, a MODIFY step) - 5 red against the first half of this fix, all green after; and the measured job file, dry-run by this branch's runner against `claude/clever-lumiere-c24d8d`'s library, now prints *1 WILL NOT RUN IN A BATCH*. A dry-run judgement of all 142 job files in `tools/jobs/` on 2026-10-12: none has a setup step above MODIFY, and 12 jobs in 10 files - `export-views-to-dwg`, `export-families`, `export-schedule-to-csv` (PUBLISH), `add-project-parameter`, `transfer-project-parameters-between-documents` (ADMIN) - had been reading WOULD RUN and now read WILL NOT RUN IN A BATCH. No Revit was needed or used: this is the runner's own judgement, before Revit is touched. |

Parameter name: topElevation"* and made nothing. **The first reading - that Revit takes a run path's Z relative to the stair's base - was measured and is wrong.** RevitAPI.xml for all eight releases, 2020 to 2027, says the path's Z is *"the base elevation for the new run (in model coordinates)"* and *"must be greater than or equal to the stairs base elevation"*, while a run's `TopElevation` and `BaseElevation`, a sketched run and a landing are relative to the stair's base. Then the setter, on that run: Top = 1200, its own reading, **refused**; Top = 1714.3, ten risers relative, **refused**; Top = 5200 **accepted and applied as relative** - 5142.9 and 30 risers, far above Level 3. A second run made at Z = base + 1200 read Base 1200 and 5 risers with no set at all. So on 2020 the setter checks a relative value against the stair's absolute base, and the path's length already gives each run its top: r - 1 treads make r risers. | **FIXED, version 2, DRAFT - run on Revit 2020.2, not signed.** A run's top is set only when it reads otherwise than wanted (`topAt`, within 5 mm); the path stays in model coordinates. No input added or changed. The opening (`z = topLevel.ProjectElevation`, [`fragment.cs`](../../brain/fragments/create-stairs/impl/any/fragment.cs) line 365) is unchanged and right: stair 1's opening in the Level 2 slab at 4000 mm was cut the same way. **Run in Project2 and KEPT, by the route the owner chose** - this chat's `revit_change` ran the main checkout's version 1, so the same kept call went through `heron_bridge_client.py fragment --write --apply` from the branch, after a rolled-back run: base Level 2, top Level 3, Monolithic Stair, U, 1100 mm runs, area 21000,17700 to 23400,21650 mm, entry south, first run west, start offset 0, cut opening. **Read back** by a separate read-only scratch fragment: stair 348438, base Level 2 at 4000, top Level 3 at 7600, 20 risers of 180 mm (runs 10 + 10, Base 0 and 1800, Top 1800 and 3600), 2 runs, 1 landing, plan extent X 21000..23400, Y 17700..21320 - inside the area; opening 348538 in floor 345912 on Level 3 over the same extent, that slab 68.400 to 65.794 m3, 2.606 m3 = 2.4 x 3.62 x 0.3; 0 warnings in the model. The kept call's answer was lost (`unknown_outcome`); the read found one new stair, not two. **The stair no longer exists:** Revit 2020 was closed at 14:42 without saving Project2 (the house chat read it in journal.0020.txt), four minutes after this read - so these numbers are what the run did, and there is no model left to look at. **Negative**, sent kept: base Level 3, top Level 2 - refused *"'Level 2' is not above 'Level 3'"*, nothing made, the stair and opening counts unchanged. **Not measured:** whether 2021 to 2027 share the 2020 setter's behaviour - the fix does not depend on it, and every fragment compiles on all eight releases. **Owed:** [Group BX](../needs-checking/group-bx.md) BX6 on a saved copy, and a signature. |
| **446** | **`CREATE_STAIRS` REFUSES EVERY STAIR TYPE WITH SIDE STRINGERS: IT LAYS EACH RUN'S TREAD WIDTH HARD AGAINST THE HALL'S SIDES, AND REVIT PUTS THE STRINGERS OUTSIDE THAT WIDTH.** Reported 2026-10-13 by the house chat on Project2 (Revit 2020.2): with stairType `Private`, Level 1 to Level 2, run width 1100 or 1050, the stair Revit made reached 50 mm past the given area on both sides across the runs - extent X 18350..20850 for an area of X 18400..20800, and X 18400..20800 for X 18450..20750 - so the read-back's "runs outside the hall" check (5 mm) refused every attempt and nothing was made; `Monolithic Stair` with the same area was accepted. **Why, from the code and the API:** RevitAPI.xml, 2020 to 2027 alike, says `StairsRun.ActualRunWidth` is *"the tread width excluding the width of independent side supports"*. The fragment sets it to `runWidth`, puts each run's centre line `width / 2` from a hall side (`across1`, `across2`) and asks for `2 x width` across (`needAcross`) - nothing allows for a support. `StairsType` names each side's support (`LeftSideSupportType`, `RightSideSupportType`) and, for an open carriage, its `LeftLateralOffset` and `RightLateralOffset`. | **FIXED IN CODE 2026-10-19 - `CREATE_STAIRS` version 4, row [1239](#), DRAFT, run rolled back only (Group HS, HS2):** the first layout is a trial whose overhang on each side of the hall is measured, then the runs are laid out that much further in - `190mm max riser 250mm going` in the Kerala house hall: 50 mm each side, extent exactly the hall's faces. What follows is the row as it was. **OPEN - recorded, not fixed: a plan-layout defect, found while fixing [445](#)'s elevations.** **The check is right and stays.** The rectangle is the clear inside of the hall and a stringer 50 mm into a wall is a clash, so refusing is the honest answer and nothing wrong is left. The repair is in the layout: inset each run by the support on its side and count both supports in `needAcross`, each width read from the type or measured off a run made in a rolled-back trial. So Level 1 was "lost" 0.43 s BEFORE its job finished and Level 2 0.36 s before: the pipe was cut, no deadline ran out (a fragment waits 180 s). A later `FILTER_ELEMENTS_BY_CATEGORY` counted 64 walls, eight floors of eight. **Not the cause: queue time eating the client's deadline.** The MCP server holds one lock per tool call (`_revit_lock`), so the nine reached Revit one at a time, each only after the one before had answered, and no queue formed in the add-in. **The hook log has nothing for these calls** (`heron-hooks.jsonl`): no hook in `.claude/settings.json` matches `mcp__heron__*`. **Who connected is not recorded anywhere** - the bridge logs that a connection was displaced, never by whom - and no Claude Code transcript on the PC has another Heron call spanning that second. **The likeliest second connection is a test sweep.** Another session started `python tools/check-gaps.py` at 10:10:33 (worktree `compassionate-wiles-fe9927`); it runs the suites one at a time in name order, and its own timings put `tests/test_mcp_serves.py` at about 10:21:02 to 10:21:36 and `tests/test_mcp_stdio.py` straight after - the two displacements fall in that window. Measured 2026-10-13 by running the 63 suites that import the bridge or server code under a scratch audit hook that refused, and recorded, any open of the live Revit's pipe (`heron.2020.33484`): exactly those two reached for it - `test_mcp_serves.py` 1,842 attempts, `test_mcp_stdio.py` 121 through the server it starts - and both still PASS, so nothing ever said so. The call is `heron_resolve` -> `_revit_version` (`mcp/server/heron_mcp_server.py`) -> `binding.sessions()` -> `bridge.discover()`, which pings every Revit in the real discovery folder. That timing is a match, not a recording: whether the sweep reached the pipe at 10:21:35 is not written anywhere. **That the suites reach a live Revit is its own defect, recorded and not fixed here.** **The defect, `revit/Heron.Bridge/BridgeServer.cs` `ListenLoopAsync`:** every accepted connection `Dispose`d the previous one whatever it was doing. One inside `Dispatch`, waiting on Revit's thread, carried on; its `WriteLineAsync` then threw on the disposed pipe and `ServeAsync`'s `catch (Exception)` swallowed it. docs/25 §3 recorded the gap on 2026-08-28 as *"the first chat loses one reply"* and left it for a machine that could compile the transport. **Reproduced without Revit**, with a scratch stand-in host compiled from the real `Heron.Bridge` and `Heron.Core` sources (one 0.5 s job at a time) and the real `heron_bridge_client`, called in the MCP server's own pattern - one lock, resolve and ping, the write, close: one chat alone, all 10 came back; the same chat while another process pinged every 150 ms, 30 calls - **not one came back**, 30 "lost", 16 of which had run; 20 connections at once - 1 came back, 19 lost, all 20 had run. **Two smaller holes of the same kind, found reading:** `RevitDispatcher.Dispatch` abandoned a job on the busy timeout while `Execute` checked `IsAbandoned` and then marked it started in a second step, so a job could be run AND reported *"Revit is busy and did not take the request"* - more likely behind a queue, because the busy clock starts at the enqueue; and `heron_config.response_timeout` was the operation timeout + 30 while the add-in answers within the busy wait + the operation wait, so a busy timeout above 30 (the settings allow up to the operation timeout) let the client give up first on a request that then ran. **Not checked:** the dispatcher race was never seen happen, only read; Revit 2021 to 2027 were compiled, not run. | **FIXED 2026-10-14 in this row's pull request - run on Revit 2020 (session 26276) on a new, unsaved project from the default template (Project1, 3,167 elements), nothing kept.** `BridgeServer`: a connection displaced while answering keeps its pipe until that answer is written, then closes (`Connection` - each move one compare-and-swap, so a request is never both run and dropped, and one read in the instant its pipe is displaced is not run at all); one displaced while waiting goes at once, as before; `PipeInstances` 2 to 16, and a full house waits for an instance rather than ending the listen loop. `RevitDispatcher`: `TryStart` and `TryAbandon` on one compare-and-swap - whichever side moves the job first wins, and a busy timeout that loses waits for the answer. `heron_config`: the client waits busy + operation + 20, still 90 at the defaults. **Measured on the stand-in with the fix:** the pinger case, all 30 came back; 20 at once, 18 or 19 came back and the 1 or 2 that did not had NOT run - their request reached an idle pipe in the instant it was displaced, which the client still calls unknown: safe, and never a lost answer for work that ran; the negative case, every third call told to fail, 7 came back ok and 3 reported `operation_failed`, none lost. `tests/test_bridge_roundtrip.py` gained three checks - a newer connection served while an answer is owed, the displaced connection still gets it, and only then is dropped - seen to FAIL on the old `BridgeServer.cs` (*"the answer owed to the displaced connection was lost: 'end of stream'"*) and pass on the fix; `tests/test_config_and_health.py` gained the busy + operation ordering, 24 failures on the old `heron_config.py` and none on the fix. The dispatcher half has no check that runs without Revit. **Run on Revit 2020, 2026-10-14, with the scratch driver and the real `heron_bridge_client`, every request sent `idempotent=False` so a lost answer could not hide behind a retry.** BEFORE, on the add-in as it stood (session 33484, a new unsaved project, Project2): twelve reads of the walls on Level 1 at once through one lock while a second connection pinged the same Revit every 150 ms - the audit holds all twelve, `ok`, on Project2, and the chat got eleven: one *"the answer was lost"* for a read Revit had answered (twice, 05:12:46 and 05:13:05 UTC, the add-in logging a displacement each time). AFTER, the fixed add-in deployed for 2020 and Revit restarted (session 26276): 13 calls at once the same way - six reads, five `create-wall` trial writes that Revit built and rolled back (`applied: false`, `rolledBack: true`, `created 1 item(s)` each), a source that cannot compile and a wall type that does not exist - **every one came back**: eleven ok, `compile_failed` and `bad_request_value` reported as failed, 19 pings meanwhile, and the add-in logged six times that a displaced connection *"is waiting on Revit and will close once its answer is written"*; the audit holds exactly those thirteen with the same results. Ten reads on ten separate connections at once - all ten came back; the morning's twelve-read shape - all twelve. A second thirteen-call run came back twelve plus one *unknown*, and the audit holds twelve entries for it: the unknown one (`read-05`) never reached Revit - the leftover described above, not a lost answer. **Read back** with `FILTER_ELEMENTS_BY_CATEGORY` (PROVEN), walls on Level 1: 0 item(s), and the model's count 3,167 as before - no trial wall was kept. **On .NET 8 and .NET 10** (the runtimes of Revit 2025 to 2027) the stand-in built from the same sources gave the same: the pinger case, all 30 came back; 20 at once, 19 came back and the one that did not had not run. **Not run:** Revit 2021 to 2027 themselves (compiled only); the dispatcher's start-or-abandon race, which no run can force. **Left open on purpose:** a request that reaches an idle pipe in the instant it is displaced is not run and still comes back as unknown - safe, rare, and closable later by telling the displaced connection, before it closes, that its next request was not run. |

| **479** | **`CREATE_VIEW_FILTER` COULD NEVER RUN: ITS `parameterId` IS AN `ElementId` THE ADD-IN BINDS BY NO NAME, SO EVERY CALL WAS REFUSED AT BINDING WHATEVER WAS TYPED - AND ITS RULE FACTORY SAT BEHIND A `#if REVIT2020` THAT AN ADD-IN BUILT BEFORE 2026-10-09 NEVER DEFINES.** The `create-view-filter` part of [row 5b-191](../FRAGMENT-ISSUES.md#): that row fixed the HINT, which now says *CANNOT BE TYPED BY NAME*, and left the fragment unreachable - `OneIdNamed` in [`RevitFragment.cs`](../../revit/Heron.Revit.Addin/RevitFragment.cs) has rules for `levelId`, `sheetId`, `phaseId`, `viewFamilyTypeId` and the like, and none for `parameterId`. Version 2's `moreRules` already found its parameters by the name Revit shows, so the fragment held the right lookup and did not use it for its own first rule. Its factories also split on an `#if` naming `REVIT2020`, `REVIT2021` and `REVIT2022`, the shape [row 5b-181](../FRAGMENT-ISSUES.md#) recorded: an add-in that passes no release symbols compiles the two-argument `CreateEqualsRule`, which 2020 to 2022 do not have. **Read in the source; no call had ever reached Revit.** | **FIXED 2026-10-15 - version 3, DRAFT until Ajmal PS signs it ([DS7](../needs-checking/group-ds.md)).** Edited, not replaced - the same card, the same FRG id. [`fragment.cs`](../../brain/fragments/create-view-filter/impl/any/fragment.cs) takes `parameterName: string`, looked up among `ParameterFilterUtilities.GetFilterableParametersInCommon` for the given categories by label (`doc.GetElement`, `LabelUtils.GetLabelFor`) or by built-in name; none or two refuses and nothing is made. `matchValue` may lead with the Filters dialog's operator - equals, does not equal, contains, does not contain, begins with, does not begin with, ends with, does not end with - as `CREATE_VIEW_FILTERS_BY_RULE` version 2 reads one; none is equals. Every factory, the first rule's and `moreRules`', is reached by REFLECTION by method name - two arguments where they exist, otherwise three with `false` - so there is no `#if` left, and 2020 to 2022 are no longer case sensitive where version 2 passed `true`. Revit's own `ElementFilterIsAcceptableForParameterFilterElement` is asked before anything is created. Nothing had ever bound `parameterId`, so no caller broke: `tools/jobs/`, `tests/` and `mcp/` hold no call, only dated job-file comments that describe the old state, left as records. **Evidence:** `tools/check-fragments-compile.py` - every fragment compiles on all eight releases. On **Revit 2020** (session 25244), in "Project2", a new unsaved Systems-template project - [`create-view-filter-project2-revit2020-2026-10-15.yaml`](../../tools/jobs/create-view-filter-project2-revit2020-2026-10-15.yaml), `batch-prove` PASS: Levels, `parameterName` Name, `matchValue` *does not contain Level 1*, red, on `{3D}` - `created` id 918728, rolled back; `parameterName` System Classification - refused, *"no parameter the filter's categories share is called "System Classification" - nothing was made"*, `created` null. Second route: the positive KEPT (id 918729), then `REPORT_VIEW_FILTERS` on `{3D}` - the filter on it, red lines and fills, filters 10 to 11 - and `TEST_VIEW_FILTER_MATCH` on both levels - matched Level 2 only. **Not checked:** `moreRules` and `combine` on a model (DS7's own row), and Revit 2021 to 2027. **2026-10-15, later:** signed by Ajmal PS and set to PROVEN on his yes. |

| **486** | **NO TOOL COULD DIMENSION TO AN ELEMENT INSIDE A LOADED LINK: EVERY WORD `CREATE_LINEAR_DIMENSION` VERSION 3 TOOK - GRID, LEVEL, PLANE, LINE, WALL, FAMILY, FACE - LOOKED ONLY IN THE HOST, AND THE OBVIOUS ROUTE, `Reference.CreateLinkReference`, HANDS BACK A REFERENCE REVIT WILL NOT DIMENSION TO.** Asked for on 2026-10-15 on a real project, Revit 2020, by the session setting up its builders-work plans: ten Generic Model cut-outs tagged in four host plans live in a LINKED model (one placed link; each publishes only CenterLeftRight, CenterFrontBack and weak references, so faces are the route, as in version 3), and the owner asked for a chain from grid C along the wall to both sides of each. Read only, never written. A grep of `brain/fragments` found no `CreateLinkReference` anywhere. **Widened, not added - version 4, the same capability and FRG id.** `between` takes one word more, in front of any other: `link LINK WORD ...`, LINK the placed link's id as Revit shows it, its unique id, or the linked model's name with or without `.rvt` (in quotes when it holds one of the words; the longest run of words before a word that names a link is the link). WORD is read exactly as version 3 reads it but inside the link's own document - its ids by the same `Id.ToString()` match, its names, its geometry read with references computed and no view (a host view cannot read a link's elements) at the host view's detail level - and every direction and point is carried into the host by the link's `GetTotalTransform`, so `face ID left` means left of the HOST's view. A link not loaded or hidden in the view is refused naming the links the host places; an id not in the link is refused, naming where it IS when it is anywhere - this model or another loaded link - so a host word on a linked id (`face 919517 bottom`) now says which link holds it - the one sentence version 3's id refusals gain, and only when the id is in a link (the refusal for a word it does not know also names `link`); a link inside a link and a link with no known word after it are refused by name. The read-back adds how many references Revit holds as linked elements (`Reference.LinkedElementId`). **MEASURED IN REVIT, NOT GUESSED - five probes on the owner's word, 2026-10-15**, on the READ path in his Revit 2020 with the real project open and handed no transaction: each made a box family, a saved link source and a host linking it, all with no window, drew every dimension in a transaction it rolled back, and closed all three. As `CreateLinkReference` hands it back, written `<link>:RVTLINK/<link type>:<element>...`, NewDimension refused every reference - "not geometric references" for a family's faces and planes and a grid, "invalid number of references" for a wall's faces - in a plan with a view template, one without, and a section. Rewritten `<link>:0:RVTLINK:<element>...` and parsed back, the same references were drawn and measured what was built: a linked box's face 2000 mm from a host grid, its centre plane 2500, a linked wall 200, a linked model line 1500. A whole element - a grid, a level, a reference plane - still failed until `:0:SURFACE` named its first surface: grid 500, level 1600, plane 1000, each as drawn (`:1:` measured -304.8 mm, nonsense). Faces read through `GetInstanceGeometry` measured right but read back as the family TYPE, so the symbol route version 3 already takes is kept. The fragment then ran 33 cases on those projects, the last after the final change: every positive as built - a mixed chain `500 + 500 + 500 + 500 + 1000`, the link rotated 90 degrees, by name and quoted - and every negative refused with nothing drawn; version 2's `grid C \| grid D` unchanged at 5000. **Compiled on all eight releases** with `tools/check-fragments-compile.py`; `CreateLinkReference`, `ParseFromStableRepresentation`, `ConvertToStableRepresentation`, `LinkedElementId`, `GetLinkDocument`, `GetTotalTransform`, `IsHidden(View)`, `GetCategoryHidden` and `Options.DetailLevel` read off the reference assemblies as the same 2020 to 2027 with `tools/api-surface --members`. Whether 2021 to 2027 hand back the same written form is NOT known: the rewrite touches only the two forms measured on 2020 and uses any other as it comes. **Routing**, 78 sentences frozen before anything was measured (the two asked for, 12 held-out ways of asking, 8 of versions 2 and 3's own job, 16 questions, 20 neighbouring requests, 20 from other domains), `main` against this branch on private stores (`c95b2fb0`, then again at `93e89aec` after `main` moved - the same answers): both asked-for sentences are declared and answer here by identity on spelling and on `model` (before: `DIMENSION_GRIDS_AND_LEVELS`, `AUDIT_MEP_OPENINGS`); 10 of the 12 held-out reach here on each backend - not *put a dimension string from grid C to the linked sleeves* (`DIMENSION_GRIDS_AND_LEVELS` on both), *add dimensions from the grid to the openings in the link* (`CREATE_GRIDS` on spelling, as on `main`), *dimension to the face of an element in the link* (`DIMENSION_ELEMENT_FACES` on `model`); on `model` version 3's *dimension the opening's width and height in this section* now comes here from `LABEL_FAMILY_DIMENSION`, where version 3 meant it to. No question moved to a write on any backend. Already on `main`, the same here and not this change's: *which dimension references a linked element* reaches this write on spelling and `model`, *what is the offset of the linked model* reaches `COPY_FROM_LINK`, and on `model` *what is the distance from grid C to the opening* reaches `CREATE_ANGULAR_DIMENSION`. One request moved between writes and is repaired: *dimension the openings in the electrical link from the grid*, declared, tipped the spelling backend's coin toss *add an angular dimension* (row 482 saw the same toss) from `CREATE_ANGULAR_DIMENSION` to `CREATE_DIMENSION` - measured with each sentence alone - so `CREATE_ANGULAR_DIMENSION` declares it now and answers it by identity on both backends (its proof covers its implementation only and stays valid). **With the re-ranker** - the owner's laptop, measured once 2.3 GB was free (cross-encoder confirmed on both runs) - the two declared sentences answer here by identity and NOTHING ELSE MOVED: all 76 other sentences answer as on `main`, so 10 of the 12 held-out stay where `main` sends them (`DIMENSION_GRIDS_AND_LEVELS`, `DIMENSION_ELEMENT_FACES` and others) - the re-ranker reads identity, capability and domain, and the identity is version 2's, left so on purpose; six questions reach writes there on `main` already (`MAXIMIZE_DATUM_EXTENTS`, `UNLOAD_LINKS`, `SPLIT_MEP_RUN`, `SET_REFERENCE_PLANE_LENGTH`, this card for *which dimensions are in this view*), unchanged. `drawing-set` step 8 names the linked form; `DIMENSION_GRIDS_AND_LEVELS`'s routing table names the route. | **WIDENED, NOT ADDED - version 4 of FRG-VIEW-101, DRAFT.** Run in Revit 2020 on scratch projects, not proved: [NEEDS-CHECKING Group EH](../needs-checking/group-eh.md) is the proof, on new projects by hand, with `tools/jobs/create-linear-dimension-link-2026-10-15.yaml` (dry run: five jobs runnable). Served whole by `heron_method("family-creation")`; a skill's text is indexed by no search (`tools/check-skill-routing.py` says why), so the line moves no routing. It rests on [row 435](#)'s version 2, which is what can see NEVER SAVED. Watching it happen is NEEDS-CHECKING [Group EI](../needs-checking/group-ei.md) EI8 - an observation, not a proof. |
| **488** | **`REPORT_FAMILY_FORMS` REFUSED TO RUN WITHOUT `which`, SO EVERY CALL OF A FIXED-FAMILY BUILD TYPED `which=all` - AND THE ADD-IN COULD NOT LET A TEXT VALUE BE LEFT OUT AT ALL.** Seen 2026-10-14 on Revit 2020, the same two monitor families as [row 487](#): every `REPORT_FAMILY_FORMS` read back passed `which=all`. The card's code already read a missing `which` as every form (`(which ?? "")`, "all or nothing for every form" in its header), but `HeronBindingNote.AbsentValue` binds an absent request value only for a bool marked `optional: true` - a text, a number or a view left out is refused ([row 227](section-5b-rows-226-250.md), [row 252](section-5b-rows-251-275.md)) - so `optional: true` on `which`, the way `SET_FAMILY_TYPE_VALUES` treats `deleteType`, would have changed nothing. | **FIXED 2026-10-16 in the add-in and the card, on the owner's choice between this and leaving `which` required - compiled for all eight releases, NOT YET RUN IN REVIT.** A card may now NAME what leaving a value out means: a need marked `optional: true` that also carries `absent: <text>` is bound, when the caller leaves it out, exactly as if the caller had typed that text - the same conversion, the same refusals - and the binding note says *which not given - "all", the card's own default (optional)*. `HeronBindingNote.DeclaredAbsent` decides it and `RevitFragment` asks it before `AbsentValue`; `heron_fragment.need_absent_text` and `need_may_be_absent` mirror it for the Python half, so `heron_validate` and the words path stop waiting on such a value. **Without `absent:` nothing changes** - row 227's three cards carry none and are refused as before. `tests/test_binding_note.py` runs the rule in its test host (six new checks) and checks the binder asks it first; against main's binder and Python three of its checks go red, and with the rule broken to ignore `optional` two of the host's do. `REPORT_FAMILY_FORMS` version 2 declares `which` `optional: true`, `absent: all`; **its `impl/` did not move, so its fingerprint is `618a2699859aa0af` before and after and `python brain/heron_fragment.py --show FRG-GEO-046` still says *proof stands*** - D-30 seals `impl/` alone, and the 2026-10-03 proof ran the same code with `which` "all", the value a left-out `which` now becomes. The add-in's typed-id rule ([row 441](#)) binds digits only to an `elementIds` id LIST, refuses the whole call when one id names nothing, and looks only in the host - so a read that must name the unknown ids and reach into a link cannot be built on it. Version 2's record also cost what [row 407](#) measured a long hand-back costs. | **WIDENED 2026-10-17 - version 3, the same capability and FRG id, DRAFT, compiled for all eight releases, NOT RUN IN REVIT.** `elementIds` is a STRING (`optional`, `absent: none`): ids as Revit shows them, comma separated, matched as text against each element's own `Id.ToString()` in one walk of the model looked in (types included) - no `ElementId` is built from a number and no `IntegerValue` is read; an id that names nothing, or a part that is not an id, lands in the new `missing` (accounting) and the rest are described; typed ids decide over the selection, and a finding says the selection was not read. `linkName` (`absent: host`) names a placed link by its id or any part of its name and the ids are looked up in that link's own document - several placements of one model are read once; two different models matching, a link not loaded, or no match look nothing up, leave the ids in `missing` and say why first; locations are the link's own coordinates with this model's beside them by `GetTotalTransform`, and tags are not read through a link. `elements` is `optional` (`EmptyWhenAbsent`) so typed ids run with nothing selected. The record holds the FIRST 50 OF N and names the next id; per element it adds the unique id, the level (`LevelId`, then the five level built-ins `READ_ELEMENT_PARAMETERS` asks, naming which answered), the owner view by name, the workset by name when the element's model is workshared, a curve's length in mm, and a placed link's document title; per parameter it sorts by name ignoring case and adds the built-in name and the RAW value per storage type, labelled as Revit's internal units. `maxChars` (`absent: 60000`, raised to 5000 below that) stops the record with a last line naming the elements written whole, the one cut and every id not written; `parameterFilter` (`absent: all`) keeps parameters whose name or built-in name holds any of its words. Still ONE string, because `RevitFragment.Describe` cuts a list to three items. **Compiled** with `tools/check-fragments-compile.py` on 2020 to 2027; every new member read off the reference assemblies as the same on all eight with `tools/api-surface --members`. **Routing**, `main` against this branch on private stores: `check-routing` identical but for one line - the new routing-table claim *"describe these elements fully"*, listed as not reached (`DESCRIBE_ELEMENTS` serves it, as on `main`) - and `check-risk-crossings` the same findings. | **513** | **HERON CANNOT SAVE THE MODEL IT IS WORKING IN, AND TWICE IN TWO DAYS A WHOLE BUILD WAS LOST BECAUSE THAT MODEL HAD NEVER BEEN SAVED.** On 2026-10-13 and 2026-10-14 a house with 9 sheets and a 32-floor tower were built in models that had never been saved, and Revit was closed: both were lost. `building-shell` and `drawing-set` each listed *"a new model has been saved under a name before the first step"* as a precondition - a state to be true, served before the steps, and not an act any step performed - so nothing asked the modeller.
