Three things stop it being built as a widening alone: it is a new kind of act for the add-in - work of its own after Heron's Undo entry closes, where each earlier ambient need came with a decision ([D-112](../DECISIONS.md), [D-114](../DECISIONS.md)); a save cannot be rolled back, so [D-55](../DECISIONS.md)'s preview needs a word on it - version 2 already writes its other document on a preview; and a Save As renames the model, which the chat's pin reads as a DIFFERENT model on Revit 2020 to 2023 and for every family, where there is no `CreationGUID` (`RevitOperations.cs` line 664) and an unsaved model has no path - so `revit_change` would answer *"Nothing has been sent to Revit"* about a model it had just saved, and aim the next call at the old title (`heron_mcp_server.py` lines 1921 and 1455; `heron_write.py` line 461). | **OPEN - A QUESTION FOR THE OWNER, NOT BUILT.** [OPEN-QUESTIONS Q-69](../open-questions/tier-2.md) holds the proposal - `SAVE_DOCUMENT` version 3 with the add-in saving once the group has closed, a preview that saves too (the only way to prove it: [D-117](../DECISIONS.md) refuses a DRAFT kept for real, and [D-119](../DECISIONS.md)'s `try-once` runs only on a model with nothing unsaved), and a pin that accepts a new title only when the reply says this run did the Save As - the evidence above, and three shapes for him to choose between. Nothing Heron does at runtime changed. **The methods ask first, 2026-10-17, DRAFT like every step of them.** `building-shell` and `drawing-set` have a step 0: `REPORT_OPEN_DOCUMENTS` (version 2 says NEVER BEEN SAVED of a project too), and when the model has no file, ask the modeller ONCE to save it, Ctrl+S under a name of their own, before step 1 - Heron cannot. Each adds that capability to its list. `family-creation`'s closing paragraph says a big build in a family never saved is saved FIRST, as step 0 asks (row 487's line, untouched). | **523** | **A FRAGMENT THAT LOOPED FOR EVER HELD REVIT UNTIL REVIT WAS ENDED: THE DISPATCHER COULD ONLY ANSWER `still_running`, AND THE STOP THAT MIGHT HAVE HELPED WAS READ ONLY BEFORE A JOB STARTED AND SET BY NOTHING.** A fragment runs on Revit's own thread inside `Execute`, and Revit offers no way into that thread from outside. `RevitDispatcher` stopped waiting after `revit.operationTimeoutSeconds` and answered *"It is still working; do not repeat the request"* - true, and of no help: the loop went on, Revit stayed frozen, and the modeller's only way out was ending Revit with the model unsaved. `HeronStop` said of itself *"Nothing can set this flag today"*, and [D-46](../decisions/D-46.md) recorded both of its gates as inert. And a fragment that catches `Exception` - much of the library does - would have swallowed any throw put in its way. | **FIXED 2026-10-17 in the add-in, [D-129](../decisions/D-129.md) - compiled for all eight releases, NOT YET RUN IN REVIT.** `HeronStackGuard` puts `HeronRunClock.Check()` at the head of every loop body and at the entry of every method and lambda; the clock trips on the dispatcher's own wait less a margin (50 s of 60) or on a stop asked during the job, and stays tripped, and `RevitFragment` asks it after every script, thrown or not, rolls the job back and answers `ran_too_long` or `stopped_mid_run`, saying whether Revit confirmed the rollback. A new `stop` operation, declared Read, is answered by the dispatcher before the queue so it reaches a Revit a runaway holds; nothing over the bridge resumes, and a restart of Revit clears it, which `HeronStop.Message` now names. `tests/test_stack_guard.py`: every loop in the library asks the clock, the rewrite adds nothing but its two calls and the braces a one-statement body needs, and guarded `while(true){}` and `try{while(true){}}catch(Exception){}` stop at about 200 ms on a 200 ms deadline. **LEFT OPEN:** `tools/check-fragments-compile.py` compiles each fragment WITHOUT the rewrite, so a guarded fragment is first compiled against a release's Revit assemblies inside Revit - the gap D-28 warns of, narrowed by the test host's token-for-token check and not closed; an expression-bodied lambda, a LINQ query and a `goto` loop carry no check; a single long Revit API call cannot be interrupted; and nothing in the MCP server sends `stop` yet. Whether a stop should survive a restart was [Q-68](../open-questions/from-master-specification-part-2.md); the owner answered the same day, [D-130](../decisions/D-130.md): a restart clears it. NEEDS-CHECKING [Group EZ](../needs-checking/group-ez.md). |
| **540** | **NO HERON TOOL COULD PUT ONE VIEW WHERE ANOTHER IS, ON EVERY SHEET IT IS ON - THE AS-BUILT LEGEND SWAP.** Found 2026-10-17 in the chat *"Shop drawings note verification"*, on a real project in Revit 2020: the shop-drawing legend NOTES sits on four sheets, a copy AS-BUILT NOTES carries the as-built wording, and at the as-built issue it has to go exactly where NOTES is on every sheet. `PLACE_VIEW_ON_SHEET` placed a view at a typed point on one sheet, `PLACE_VIEWS_ON_SHEET` laid views out on a grid, `PLACE_SCHEDULE_ON_SHEET` placed a schedule at a point - none takes a view off, none reads where another view sits, so the swap was done by hand sheet by sheet. Measured on `main` `5fce1f2`: none of *"swap the NOTES legend for AS-BUILT NOTES on all sheets"*, *"replace this view on the sheets with that one"* or *"put this legend where the old one is"* reached a sheet-placing tool on any of four backends - they went to `LIST_DATES_ON_SHEETS`, `SET_SHEET_TITLE_BLOCK`, `LIST_SHEET_SETS`, `PLACE_VIEWS_ON_SHEET` and `CREATE_LEGEND_VIEW`, which makes a NEW legend. **Why `PLACE_VIEW_ON_SHEET` and not the plural:** its `views` is typed by name; the plural's views arrive only from a chain or the selection, and its whole point is a grid. **API, read with `tools/api-surface` one release at a time:** `Viewport.LabelOffset` and `ViewSchedule.IsSplit` exist from 2022; `Viewport.ViewId` has a SETTER from 2023 - not used, because 2020 to 2022 cannot and one path keeps every release the same run. | **WIDENED 2026-10-17 - DRAFT, NOT RUN IN REVIT (NEEDS-CHECKING [Group FC](../needs-checking/group-fc.md)).** `PLACE_VIEW_ON_SHEET` version 2 (was PROVEN; version 1's proof kept on the card as a record, stale): a new optional `oldViewId` - `optional: true`, `absent: none`, so no new required input - and `sheetId` may now be left out the same way, meaning every sheet the old view is on. A viewport is replaced, not changed: a new one at the old box centre, the old viewport type, rotation and (2022 and later) title position, then the old one deleted and its detail number and pin carried over; a schedule takes the old one's corner. `at` is an offset in mm, `0,0,0` the job. Each sheet is a SubTransaction - all or nothing, the old view kept where a sheet fails - and read back: new `swapped` lines (sheet number, old gone, new there, centre or corner difference in mm, viewport type) and new `notDone` reasons; `refused` holds the sheets left unchanged. More than 0.1 mm off is not kept. A split schedule (2022 and later) and a title block's revision schedule are refused. The add-in resolves `oldViewId` by view name (`RevitFragment.OneIdNamed`, one name added). Compiled: the fragment on all eight releases 2020 to 2027 (`tools/check-fragments-compile.py`) and the add-in on all eight (`tools/check-compile.py`). Proof plan `tools/jobs/place-view-on-sheet-swap-2026-10-17.yaml`, dry-run clean: version 1's placing job, a schedule on one sheet, and THE PROOF - a legend on two sheets with different viewport types; the negative is a drafting view already on another sheet, which must change nothing. **Routing, measured on four backends against `main` `cfde9bd8`** (this branch merged with it) - lexical (CI's), the trained model `potion-base-8M`, and each with the cross-encoder re-ranker - each sentence through `heron_retrieve.find`, fresh scratch stores: the three sentences above reach this card by identity on all four; of eleven ways of asking the swap, 5 reach it on lexical, 6 with the re-ranker, 5 on the model, 6 on the model with the re-ranker (0 or 1 on main). **No question moved onto this card against main, and no change request moved onto a read**; `tools/check-risk-crossings.py` answers the same on both. *"is this view already on a sheet"* reached this card with the re-ranker on main as well; `LIST_VIEWS_ON_SHEET` version 2 (row 5b-560, merged the same day) answers it with `viewName`, so it is declared there and reaches that read on all four. Three measured choices: the version 2 line in `purpose:` says *replace*, not *put*, because *put* lifted this card past `SET_SELECTION` for *"put them on the screen"* on the trained model and failed `tests/test_embed.py` (0.381 against 0.379); the job's long explanation is a comment, not `purpose:`, because in `purpose:` it pulled *"show me the sheets with the notes legend"* and *"how many sheets have the notes legend"* off a read onto this change on the model backend and took *"add the view to the drawing sheet"* from #2 to #15 by keywords; and two more sentences (*"swap the legend on all the sheets"*, *"replace the legend on every sheet with the as-built one"*) were tried and left out, because they pulled *"is the AS-BUILT NOTES legend on any sheet"* and *"where is the NOTES legend placed"* onto it. **Still open, recorded not fixed:** (a) *"has this view been placed on a sheet"* reaches this card with the re-ranker on main and branch alike - not declared on `LIST_VIEWS_ON_SHEET`, whose card records that *placed* in its indexed text pulled change requests onto it; *"is this legend already placed"* reaches `CREATE_LEGEND_VIEW` everywhere, as on main; (b) some other ways of asking the swap still reach `CREATE_LEGEND_VIEW`, `SET_SHEET_TITLE_BLOCK` or `CREATE_REVISION`, as on main; (c) `check-routing` lets this card outrank `PLACE_VIEWS_ON_SHEET`' own *"put these views on a sheet"* by keywords - it is answered by identity, so `find` still sends it home on all four backends. Owed: the Revit proof, in a LOCAL session on the owner's PC on a scratch project - never the real 4355 model - then Ajmal PS signs it and says whether it goes PROVEN. |
| **545** | **`MANAGE_SHEET_SETS` SET THE PRINT DIALOG'S RANGE TO *SELECTED VIEWS/SHEETS* AND NEVER PUT IT BACK, SO AN UNDO LEFT REVIT'S PRINT DIALOG CHANGED - AND `LIST_SHEET_SETS` NAMED TWO SETS HOLDING THE SAME 8 SHEETS WITHOUT A WORD.** Two findings of 2026-10-14 on Ajmal PS's Revit 2020 job model 4355-BHVD-3D-21S10-BL001A, carried here with [row 466](#)'s build because all three are one change to two cards. **The print range.** `ViewSheetSetting` throws unless `PrintManager.PrintRange` is `Select`, so version 1 set it - and `PrintManager` is application state, not document state: RevitAPI.xml calls `PrintRange` *"the global print setting property used for print operations on any document"*. Nothing put it back, so after a rolled-back or undone run the Print dialog stayed on Selected views/sheets. Version 1's own `cases.yaml` recorded this as how it works (*"the print RANGE still changed"*), not as a defect. **The duplicate sets.** The same day a seventh set was made on BL001A with `MANAGE_SHEET_SETS` holding the same 8 sheet numbers as the first; `LIST_SHEET_SETS` (row 465) listed both in full and said nothing about it, so a person choosing a set before an issue had to compare two lists of 8 by eye. **Not checked here:** whether the Print dialog reads the range back from the print manager it was put back on, or needs `PrintManager.Apply()` (Group FA, FA4); whether rename, delete, add and remove leaving the set they worked on as the dialog's CURRENT set is a defect too (FA8 - version 1 did it for rename and delete, and nothing here changes it); where an added sheet lands in a set ordered by hand on 2023+ (FA7). Three old job files - `tools/jobs/list-as-setup.yaml`, `modify-never-run.yaml` and `modify-round-2.yaml`, Revit 2024 rounds of 2026-09-09 - pass `mode: "add"` or `"remove"`, which version 1 refused; version 2 reads them as modes. All three are `write: true`, so a re-run rolls back, and they name `HERON SET Z1`, which add and remove never create. They are run records and are left as they are. | **WIDENED 2026-10-17 - `MANAGE_SHEET_SETS` version 2 and `LIST_SHEET_SETS` version 2, both DRAFT, compiled for all eight releases, NOT RUN IN REVIT.** `LIST_SHEET_SETS` version 1 was PROVEN the same day ([row 465](#), EA1); this change makes that proof stale, and Ajmal PS chose to merge it and take the card back to DRAFT, with FA5 as its re-proof. [`MANAGE_SHEET_SETS`](../../brain/fragments/manage-sheet-sets/fragment.yaml) (FRG-SHT-015) goes back to DRAFT on Ajmal PS's yes (his starting the task); version 1's proof stays in the card as its record and `check-signatures` lists it STALE. **Add and remove** ([row 466](#)): the set is made current, its members read back, the elements given merged in or taken out by `ElementId`, then `ViewSheetSetting.Save()`. RevitAPI.xml says `Save()` throws on an unchanged set, so a call that changes nothing is refused first, in words - and so is a remove that would leave the set empty (that is mode `delete`). A `Save()` that fails is followed by `Revert()`. No new required input: they use `elements` and `setName`. New outputs `changed` (int, result), `unchanged` (ids given and left as they were, accounting) and `printRange` (accounting). **The range** is read before it is set and put back in a `finally`, whatever happened in between, on the same print manager - `Apply()` is NOT called, because RevitAPI.xml says it pushes every local print setting to the global ones for all documents, a wider change than version 1 made. `printRange` says *put back to Current window*, *already Selected views/sheets*, or *NOT put back* with what it was. If the Revit run shows the dialog still on Selected views/sheets after *put back*, the API's way to reach it is `Apply()`, and calling it is Ajmal PS's choice (FA4). [`LIST_SHEET_SETS`](../../brain/fragments/list-sheet-sets/fragment.yaml) (FRG-SHT-032, still DRAFT): sets holding exactly the same sheets and views, as sets of `ElementId` by the type's own equality - not by name, count or print order - are each marked *SAME sheets and views as* the other, paired in a new `sameSets` (*"A = B"*), and named in a finding with their counts. Empty and unreadable sets are not compared. **Evidence, none of it Revit:** every new member - `PrintManager.PrintRange` get and set, `ViewSheetSetting.Save()` and `Revert()`, `IViewSheetSet.Views`, `ViewSet.IsEmpty`, `ElementId.Equals` and `GetHashCode` - looked up on all eight releases with `tools/api-surface --members`, the same signature 2020-2027; `check-fragments-compile` every fragment on every release it claims, 2020-2027; `check-api-surface` OK. The add and remove merge and the SAME grouping were copied verbatim into a scratch console program with stand-in types and run: an add of four ids into a set of three puts in two, leaving out the one already there and the one given twice, remove refuses an empty result and a set none of the given are in, three sets of the same members in different orders group as one, a set with one view more does not. **Routing, measured on fresh scratch stores through `heron_brain.lookup` (the function `heron_lookup` calls), both backends (`model`: potion-base-8M, no re-ranker; lexical: the same Python with user site-packages off, as CI runs), each behind the 2020 and the 2024 wall, every fragment's utterances, every quoted sentence in a routing table, every skill utterance, the owner's questions, and 27 about sheet sets written before any wording was chosen - about 4,960 sentences.** Run against `main` `76006767`, `5f76d96e`, `6efcde61` and, finally, `a673d8d2`; the final result: **on `model` 4 sentences moved, on lexical 6** (leaving out a scratch-script artifact, the bare word *remove*). *add these sheets to the set* leaves `CREATE_SHEETS` for this card on both - [row 466](#)'s routing half - and *take these sheets out of the set* reaches it on both; Those two are caused by this card going from PROVEN to DRAFT, measured by bisection: putting `heron-status: PROVEN` back with every other line of the branch kept puts both back, and no change to any wording does. On .NET 8 and later an assembly name is loaded once into Revit's default context, so Heron's 4.8 C# layer ran on AJ Tools' 4.14 core and a method it expects was not there. Revit's journal had said so first, line 1438: *"Addin's module Microsoft.CodeAnalysis of version 4.8.0.0 conflicts with same preloaded module of version 4.14.0.0"*. **It is a race, not a fixed state.** On Revit 2027.3 the same afternoon (PID 6096, "EQ-2027 Snowdon Towers Sample HVAC", 9,813 elements) AJ Tools had loaded all four of its 4.14 files, the C# layer included, before Heron's warm-up ran - so Heron compiled on AJ Tools' compiler (warm-up 2708 ms) and `list-sheets` answered its 17 sheets: the right answer, from a compiler Heron never shipped or tested, in an order that is not Heron's to choose. Revit 2020 and 2024 (.NET Framework) print the same conflict line and are not affected: there both versions load side by side (warm-ups 4941 ms and 5368 ms). [docs/07 §10.9](../07-installation-and-update.md) item 1 calls that line harmless; on 2026 it was not. **Seen on the way, recorded only:** Revit 2026.5 on this PC runs on .NET 10 - all 87 `*.runtimeconfig.json` in its folder say `net10.0`, and the process loaded `coreclr.dll` from `Microsoft.NETCore.App\10.0.12` - while Heron's 2026 build is `net8.0-windows` and loaded on it by roll-forward. Not run: Revit 2025 (not installed on this PC), and Revit 2026 with AJ Tools removed. | **REPAIRED IN CODE 2026-10-17 - NOT YET RUN IN REVIT** ([Group GW](../needs-checking/group-gw.md)). Recorded open by the session that found it, which was running load checks. The repair keeps Heron's compiler out of the shared context on 2025 and later without a manifest change: [`HeronScriptHost`](../../revit/Heron.Revit.Addin/HeronScriptHost.cs) - the one door the add-in uses to the compiler, and the only place its version branch lives - loads `Heron.Scripting.dll` (`HeronScriptEngine.cs` and `HeronStackGuard.cs`, built as an assembly of their own) into a load context of Heron's own, where `Heron.Scripting` and every `Microsoft.CodeAnalysis*` name come from Heron's folder and every other name - the Revit API, the add-in, `Heron.Core`, the runtime - from the default context, so a fragment still sees the types Revit hands it. On 2025 and later the add-in has no compile-time Roslyn reference at all; on 2020 to 2024 the engine is built into the add-in and called directly, as before. Measured without Revit the same day by [`tests/test_script_isolation.py`](../../tests/test_script_isolation.py), on .NET 10 against Roslyn 4.14 and on .NET 8 against 4.13: with another add-in's core loaded first the old binding threw this row's exact `TypeLoadException`, with all four loaded first it compiled on the other add-in's compiler, and with Heron first it shut the other add-in out of its own (`Assembly with same name is already loaded`) - while the door compiled and ran on Heron's 4.8 in all four cases, the script reaching the host's own static, globals object and run clock, and the default context left holding exactly the Roslyn it held before. With the load context made to hand every name to the default context - the binding before the repair - the four door cases went red. Every project compiles on all eight releases. Not run: any of it in Revit - Group GW asks for Revit 2026 and 2027 with AJ Tools still installed, and 2020 and 2024 unchanged. The state as it was recorded that afternoon: *open - recorded, not repaired* (the session that found it was running load checks; a change to `revit/` is its own job). Every Revit from 2025 on is exposed wherever another add-in loads the C# compiler before Heron does. The repair belongs in `revit/`: Heron's compiler kept out of the shared context on the .NET releases, for example in its own `AssemblyLoadContext` - Revit 2025 has no manifest isolation, and 2026's `<ManifestSettings>` must never reach 2025 or older (the `revit-version-support` skill). It is proved by EQ5 run again with AJ Tools still installed, and by a 2027 run whose loaded modules show Heron's own compiler. Until then nothing Heron does through a fragment works in Revit 2026 on the owner's laptop. |

No new capability and no new id. Before the write each face is measured off the element's geometry (its outermost point that way; hidden geometry only when it has no visible geometry); after the write and a regeneration it is measured again, the element is moved back by exactly the difference with `ElementTransformUtils.MoveElement` in the SAME transaction - **one undo entry** - and measured a third time; a face not back within 0.1 mm throws and every row is taken back. A sign with nothing to go on does not vote; none voting, or two disagreeing, refuses the element naming what each found. Refused before anything is written: pinned, in a model group, no geometry, a word that is no face, a missing or sheet-like view. By id rows a refusal stops the run (all or nothing, as version 2); by key it refuses that row. readBack gains each element's `face` (before, how far the write moved it, the move, now, off by, and the signs); resultJson gains `keepFace`. **Left out it is version 2, statement for statement:** every new line is behind `faceKeeper`, which is null, so resultJson, written, stale, refused and findings are version 2's; the reply's list of what the fragment left gains one line, `faceKeeper (null)`. The rollback is right and the sentence is not: nobody was asked. What a repair would be: a fifth answer - the box could not be shown - with its own sentence, kept as safe as Closed. Until row 5b-497 the only door a chat had for this EXECUTE fragment was `revit_change`, which runs it inside Heron's transaction, where Revit changes no view: *"The active view can only be changed when there is no open transaction"* (RevitAPIUI.xml 2024, `UIDocument.ActiveView` and `RequestViewChange`), measured for `RequestViewChange` on Revit 2020 (row 5b-456). Show moves to another view whenever the one in front cannot see the elements; whether Show itself is refused inside a transaction is not measured. Show also opens no view by name: in a family it zooms whatever view is in front, usually Ref. Level, and the API offers no way to open a named view and zoom it in one call - `RequestViewChange` happens only when the call has ended. `EXPORT_VIEW_IMAGE` (FRG-VIEW-073, PROVEN v1): nothing refuses a family either - views by name through the add-in's binder ("View 1", "Front"), `CanBePrinted`, `ExportImage` - and it exports a view in whatever visual style the view has; it shades nothing itself. Both proved in a project only. | **FIXED IN THE METHOD 2026-10-17 - NOT RUN IN REVIT. No fragment changed.** Neither tool refuses a family, and the door the zoom needed - no transaction open - is `revit_show`, merged the same day (row 5b-497, [D-125](../DECISIONS.md)); so `ZOOM_TO_ELEMENTS` stays version 1, PROVEN and untouched. A version 2 declaring `editScopeFailures` was written first in this change and dropped when `revit_show` arrived: D-125 keeps that switch for a fragment that opens its own transactions, and the show door refuses it. `EXPORT_VIEW_IMAGE` is NOT changed and NOT used: [D-123](../DECISIONS.md) made `revit_view_image` the read-only way a chat looks at a view - into the chat, no switch, nothing kept - and named `EXPORT_VIEW_IMAGE` (PUBLISH, files for other people) as the route not to take. `family-creation` step 13 now LOOKS before the modeller is told it is done: `OPEN_VIEW` "View 1" through `revit_show`; `SET_VIEW_PROPERTIES` "Shaded with Edges", detail Fine, through `revit_change`, so a part shown only at Fine is in the picture; `SELECT_FAMILY_FORMS` forms "solid" through `revit_read` and then at once `ZOOM_TO_ELEMENTS` through `revit_show` with `expect_from` "select-family-forms"; then `revit_view_image` of "View 1" (and of "Front" for a product with a front), and the agent looks at the picture in the chat for a part on the wrong side, missing, or off the body. Where `revit_show` is not offered or Revit's Heron does not know it yet, the modeller is asked to open View 1 and press ZF. It says the picture is a read - no switch, nothing in the family changes - and is for the agent to look at, never passed on. `revit_view_image`'s element-id way is not used: it takes ids as digits, and the family form tools hand back unique ids. Both paths copy exactly as version 2. Onto a NAMED workset the copy is made with that workset ACTIVE - Revit's own place for every copy and everything riding with it, as a modeller does it by hand - and the active workset put back in a `finally`. For `source` each copy's `ELEM_PARTITION_PARAM` is set to the value its ORIGINAL's holds, as `SET_ELEMENT_WORKSET` writes it: the offset path straight after the copy, the onto-levels path while the copies still stand a kilometre aside (step 3a). **No id is read as a number** - a first draft read `WorksetId.IntegerValue`, valid on every release, and `tests/test_api_changes.py` went red on it, because its detector cannot tell that from the `ElementId.IntegerValue` 2026 removed and no fragment in the library writes `.IntegerValue` in code; a workset is now a `WorksetId` compared with `==`. For `source`, when every original - each given element and its dependents (`GetDependentElements`: a door in a given wall, a panel) - is on one workset, every copy goes there; otherwise each copy is matched by category and type, then by its plan place less the step; a copy of a kind no original is (a sketch plane) stays where Revit made it and is counted; two originals on different worksets it could be a copy of are never guessed between - the whole call is taken back. A copy whose workset is read-only follows its host or view and is counted, never written. `onWorksets` (new, result, ONE string) READS every copy's workset back from the model - the active workset named, a count per workset, how many of those written hold the one asked for - and a copy whose workset can be set that does not hold it takes the call back. A choice in a model that is not workshared, a name the model has no user workset for (its user worksets listed), or a named workset closed in this session, is refused before anything is copied - the turn's rule, a value that would be ignored is refused instead. **Compiled** with `tools/check-fragments-compile.py` on 2020 to 2027; every new member read off the reference assemblies as the same on all eight with `tools/api-surface --members`. **Routing**, `main` 7600676 against this branch on private stores, the trained model with and without the re-ranker: the one utterance added - the owner's sentence - is answered by identity on both; `score-routing` and `check-risk-crossings` identical on both; `check-skill-routing` one runner-up rank on both (*"work out the airflow for this room"*, winner `READ_SPACE_LOADS` unchanged). Of six held-out paraphrases of the owner's sentence, none declared, `COPY_ELEMENTS` is first for 3 without the re-ranker (0 on `main`) and 4 with it (4 on `main`). | **882** | **A REVIEW OF THE ONE-STEP INSTALLER AFTER IT MERGED (#558) FOUND IT WOULD BUILD PACKAGES FROM SOURCE ON A PYTHON NEWER THAN THE LOCK WAS MEASURED ON, AND SAID SEVERAL THINGS THAT WERE NOT TRUE.** Found 2026-10-17 by a code review of `afec42b7`, read in the source, not run on Windows. (1) `PythonSetup` accepted any Python from 3.11 up and pip had no `--only-binary`: on Python 3.15 - not one the lock's wheels were downloaded for - pip would fetch the hashed source archives of PyYAML, cffi, pydantic-core and rpds-py, need a compiler a modeller has not got, and fetch build tools no hash checks. (2) The build step said *"heron-ready built what a first chat would otherwise build"* whatever heron-ready answered - a crash, or a store left alone, included. (3) A pip failure was reported by pip's LAST line, which for a real hash mismatch is `Got 5f3e...`, not the error; and every pip failure was told to check the internet, a virtual environment and a hash mismatch included. (4) A Python that started and failed, or did not answer in time, was told to install Python with winget. (5) `heron-install --from` made its work folder under temp BEFORE the Store-Claude check, so from inside the app the refusal's *"Nothing was changed"* was untrue. (6) `Run()`'s comment and Group EP said every step stops the run; the build never did. (7) The two doors wired the same four lines each, and the shared runner read its buffers on the ceiling path while a reader could still append. | **FIXED 2026-10-17 in code, DRAFT - NOT RUN on Windows.** Python 3.11 to 3.14 only (`NewestMinor`, said in the sentence before Install and in `heron-install --help`), and `--only-binary=:all:`; the build step's line follows heron-ready's exit code (0 built, 1 listed something to fix, else stopped unexpectedly with its own last line); pip's cause is its last `ERROR:` line, with advice matched to a network failure, a hash mismatch or a virtual environment; a Python that started is told what it said, a hung one that it did not answer; the Store-Claude check comes before anything touches the PC; `RunAndSave` is the one method both doors call; one lock guards both buffers. `tests/Heron.Installer.TestHost` (`PythonSetupChecks`) holds each - five of its new checks seen to FAIL with the fixes undone - and `tests/test_heron_ready.py` now holds the NOT READY summary the installer reads, so a reworded heron-ready goes red. **Recorded, not built:** (a) **for the owner** - heron-install sets up Python by default and the window has no way to decline it while installing the connector; docs/28 says the Dependency Agent is *"Confirm, never automatic"*, and whether the sentence before Install is enough confirmation, or both doors need an explicit yes (a tick box, an opt-in flag), is Ajmal PS's to decide; (b) `python` is started through CreateProcess, which never runs a `.bat` shim such as pyenv-win's, so such a PC is told no Python answered - far from the modeller this is for; (c) closing the window during the Python half leaves pip running and writes no log - the same as closing it during the engine's wait for Revit; (d) the final `--check` still runs after a build that said READY, because a final check is what was asked for. Windows runs: [Group EP](../needs-checking/group-ep.md). |

| **917** | **`VALIDATE --WRITE` RAN A PROOF'S SETUP IN ANOTHER ORDER THAN IT WAS TYPED - EVERY STEP THAT ONLY READS FIRST, EVERY STEP THAT CHANGES THE MODEL LAST, INSIDE THE FRAGMENT'S OWN GROUP - SO A SELECTOR TYPED AFTER A STEP THAT MAKES ELEMENTS LOOKED BEFORE THEY EXISTED.** Found 2026-10-17 on Revit 2020 ([row 5b-520](section-5b-rows-401-425.md), finding 1): a setup that made two text notes followed by `select-by-category-name` handed on 0 (`elements from ... (0)`), and a view a setup step makes could not be named in a later read step's view value (*"No view called ..."*). The cause is in `cmd_validate` (`mcp/client/heron_bridge_client.py`): `arrange` runs each READ or EXECUTE step as its own `run_fragment_read` before the phase, and `deferred_setup` puts each MODIFY step inside the fragment's own `run_fragment_write` request, where `RevitFragment.RunSetupSteps` runs it after them. Nothing said so: the usage read *"Repeatable, in order"* and the `fragment-proving` skill *"re-run before each phase, in order"*. **Six job rows in `tools/jobs/` were typed in that order** (every job file scanned 2026-10-17), and four were written on the belief that the read step finds what the change step made: `delete-revision`, `reorder-revision` and `merge-revision` in `sheet-issues-revisions-test-copy-2026-09-28.yaml` each select `HERON PROOF second`, which their own `create-revision` step makes, and `slope-roof-edges-test-projject-2026-09-24.yaml` says *"THE ROOF IS BUILT BY THE SETUP, NOT FOUND"* and then selects roofs with `select-by-category-name` and `set-selection`. The other two - `edit-revision` in that file and in `sheet-issues-revisions-renumber-2026-09-28.yaml` - select a revision the model already held, so the order changed nothing for them. No run record or proof draft of any of the six is in the repository, and all five fragments are DRAFT. | **FIXED 2026-10-17 on the Python side, by refusing, not reordering - tested without Revit, NOT RUN IN REVIT.** `setup_order_refusal` in `mcp/client/heron_bridge_client.py`: on `validate --write`, a setup step that runs as a read (READ or EXECUTE) typed after a step at MODIFY is refused before Revit is asked anything, naming both steps, the risk of each and why. `tools/batch-prove.py`'s `job_refusal` asks the same rule, so `--dry-run` says `REFUSED` where it said `WOULD RUN`. A setup typed read-first runs exactly as before, and now in the order typed. **Refused rather than reordered** because the typed order is a claim about what each step will see; running a read step inside the group in its typed place would need the add-in to do it, which is C# and needs a real Revit to prove. `tests/test_setup_order.py` holds it: 11 of its 23 checks failed on the code before - the fake Revit was sent the selector first and then the write carrying `create-text-note` - and all pass after, seen both ways with the fix stashed. The usage text and the [`fragment-proving`](../../.claude/skills/fragment-proving/SKILL.md) skill now say the rule. **What it costs:** the six job rows above read `REFUSED` at `--dry-run`. The four built on the belief need a new arrangement - the change step leaving the value the fragment takes, with `--keep-chain`. The synthetic replies in [`tests/test_time_family_build.py`](../../tests/test_time_family_build.py) are written with real newlines and no wrapper, which is why the suite passes. [`tests/test_time_family_build.py`](../../tests/test_time_family_build.py) section 9 gives it a `revit_change` reply in exactly that shape, as a string and as one text part: 8 checks fail on the old `_text_of` and pass on the new one, and a wrapped reply with no count is still NOT MEASURED. Reproduced 2026-10-17 in a cloud container (model2vec 0.10.0, `minishlab/potion-base-8M`; sentence-transformers 6.1.0, torch 2.14.1, `cross-encoder/ms-marco-MiniLM-L-6-v2`), each backend forced in-process - `heron_embed._MODEL_CACHE[:] = [None]` for no model, `heron_rerank._CACHE[:] = [None]` for no re-ranker. **It is the re-ranker, not the trained model:** on `main` `2ae76a15` (before [D-134](../DECISIONS.md#d-134--the-re-ranker-reads-a-cards-first-declared-sentences-beside-its-identity-carried-in-the-store), #616), `2c1b4786` (after it) and `5d99a553`, the suite passes on the spelling backend and on the model alone, and fails the two live-lookup checks with the re-ranker over either, naming `['curtain-wall', 'duct-layout']` and `['curtain-wall', 'building-shell']`. The owner's PC has both. **Why.** `heron_lookup` names a method only beside a lookup whose five candidates include a capability it needs ([`heron_skill.methods_for`](../../brain/heron_skill.py)). For both sentences the only such candidate is `CREATE_LINEAR_DIMENSION`, held 2nd or 3rd by the words route alone - #1 there through the sentence row 650 declared, *"dimension the builders work layout from the grid"*; nearness never finds it. The re-ranker re-reads the top twenty from a card's identity, capability and domain and, since D-134, its first eight utterances. That sentence is twelfth, so it read a card about grids, levels and wall faces and put it 7th and 8th (spelling) and 13th and 15th (model) on `2ae76a15`, and 13th and 15th, 17th and 19th on `2c1b4786`. No candidate linked the method, and methods sharing ONE word filled the list: `curtain-wall` through *"set"* in the first sentence and *"this"* in the second, `building-shell` through *"this"*, `duct-layout` through *"layout"*. *"this"* counts only because `heron_skill._words` makes it *"thi"* before it drops plain words - row 650's finding (1), still open. `builders-work-layout` shares *builder*, *work* and *layout* with both sentences, and its phrases keep away from *"this"* ([row 741](#)) where `curtain-wall` says *"this curtain wall"* three times - it was never out-ranked, it was not in the list. The two checks that passed give `methods_for` the capabilities the spelling backend returned, `CREATE_LINEAR_DIMENSION` among them, so they cannot see a re-ranker. Row 650 measured its routing on two backends - spelling and model2vec `potion-base-8M`, neither with the re-ranker - and said so: *"Not measured: the owner's trained model and re-ranker"*. | **FIXED 2026-10-17 - the card is DRAFT; measured in a container, NOT on the owner's PC.** The card's identity gains *"- for builders work too"*: *"dimension between these grids, levels, planes, lines or wall faces, on this view - for builders work too"*. Versions 3 and 4 were built for that job - builders work openings dimensioned from the grid ([rows 482](#) and [486](#)) - and the identity is the one line every backend, the re-ranker included, always reads; it now names the job the way `REPORT_SPACE_ENVELOPE`'s names its load calculation. No sentence was added or reworded, and the implementation and fingerprint are untouched. **Tried and not taken, measured on `2c1b4786`:** moving row 650's sentence into the first eight, which D-134 makes the obvious lever - both sentences then named the method, but its words *"builders work layout"* took the QUESTION *"which openings are in the builders work layout"* from `SELECT_OPENINGS`, a read, to this write on both re-ranked backends. *"- for a builders work layout too"* sent *"set up the electrical layout"* and *"dimension the sprinkler layout from the grid"* to this card on the spelling backend; this card dimensions, it lays nothing out. Loosening `methods_for`'s link was not tried: that link is D-01's data, not this row's to change. **The test** gains one check CI can run: a capability `builders-work-layout` needs says *"builders work"* in the passage a re-ranker is given, built as `heron_search.index` stores it. It reads the cards, not a ranking, so it fails on `main` whatever is installed - seen on the spelling backend and on the owner's - and passes here; the two live checks are unchanged and pass on all four backends. **Measured** `main` `5d99a553` against this branch in detached worktrees (`ab77d5fb`, merged after, changed one card's comment only), fresh private stores, `heron_retrieve.find` and `heron_brain.lookup` with no Revit wall, one thread each, a run refused if any ranked answer named another backend, on 4,224 sentences: every card's utterances and identity, every method's utterances, `tests/test_routing_phrases.py`'s lists, `tools/check-risk-crossings.py`'s questions, and 45 probes on the words this touches, written before the branch was measured. **Spelling, and the model alone:** no winner and no method moved; `find` moved only for the identity's own old and new text, both still this card - the old by ranking now, the new by identity; three and two sentences changed a runner-up in their five. **With the re-ranker, over spelling and over the model:** 15 winners and 15 method lists moved on each, every one a builders work probe or `builders-work-layout`'s own sentence: the winner is now this card, and the method is named in every one of those lists - first for the owner's two sentences and its own three; no other card's or method's sentence moved on any backend. Two moved onto a write, both asking for the job - *"do the builders work for this layout"* and *"... for the layout"* - from `REPORT_SPRINKLER_LAYOUT_SPACES`, a read that is not this job. **No question moved onto a write:** the question-set sentences answered by a write are 11, 4, 10 and 14 - spelling, the model, spelling with the re-ranker, the model with it - on both trees. *"is the builders work layout dimensioned"* goes from one write to another (`DIMENSION_GRIDS_AND_LEVELS` or `DIMENSION_WALL_OPENINGS` to this card). *"which openings are in the builders work layout"* keeps `SELECT_OPENINGS` on both re-ranked backends but is now offered this MODIFY method beside it, and on the model with the re-ranker so is *"how many builders work openings are there"* - row 650's finding (3), `methods_for` not reading D-128's ceiling, still open. `check-routing`'s words-route diagnostic now puts `CREATE_DIMENSION` first for this card's *"dimension from this to that"* (the longer identity weighs each word less); `find` still answers it here, by identity. `check-routing` and `check-intrusion` exit 0 on both trees. `tools/score-routing.py --sentences` (#621), on `ab77d5fb` against this branch, 304 of them tagged - the probes, every card and method sentence saying *dimension*, *builders*, *work*, *layout* or *grid*, and both question lists - agrees: no answer changed on spelling or on the model alone; 15 changed on each re-ranked backend, every one neutral and none bad; questions reaching a write 13, 6, 12 and 18 of 108, in the same order, on both trees; wanted answers reached 156 of 156 on both. **Found on the way, recorded, not repaired:** a sentence past a card's eighth is invisible to the re-ranker - 51 of 571 cards declared more than eight on `2c1b4786` - and nothing checks which of those a routing row or a method's link depends on. Owed: `python tests/test_brain_reachable.py` on the owner's PC, with his model and re-ranker. Every move that day did the same, refused ones too: `-0075-a4ea` beside `-0074-194c`, `-0115-f43d` beside `-0114-5244`. `RevitDispatcher.Dispatch` mints the job's id and writes its line from it; `RevitOperations` calls `RevitWrite.Run(app, request, op)` without it, and `ExecuteMove` mints a second - `var workflow = HeronIdentity.NewWorkflowId();` - for its own record and for `Failed`. `HeronIdentity`'s docstring calls the workflow id the key that ties one sentence to every element touched (Golden Rule 10, [5b-6](section-5b-rows-001-025.md)). The detailed entry is complete on its own, which is what G4 asked; the join is what is lost. **NOT checked:** whether any other operation that writes its own audit record mints its own id the same way | **OPEN - recorded, not fixed.** Owed: hand the job's id to `RevitWrite.Run` and record under it - an edit to `RevitWrite.cs`, which stales every golden move case resting on it |

Read from Claude's shell afterwards: `mcp-2.2.0.dist-info` and `mcp-2.3.0.dist-info` are both seen, and `importlib.metadata.version("mcp")` answers **2.2.0**; every file under `mcp`, `cryptography`, `pydantic` and `pydantic_core` that exists in both places reads byte for byte as the COPY's (249, 190, 212 and 8 files), and three files only the real folder has show through - `pydantic\_internal\_internal_dataclass.py`, its `.pyc`, and `pydantic_core\_pydantic_core.pyi`. So Claude runs the copy's code with a few of the lock's files laid in, under the lock's version number. `heron-ready --check` from Claude's shell said READY, and `tests/test_mcp_serves.py` PASSED there in 51 s. **NOT checked:** a pair whose stray file is imported; what pip run from inside does to such a pair. | **OPEN - two sources disagree, and Ajmal PS resolves it.** [WHAT-TO-INSTALL §1b](../WHAT-TO-INSTALL.md) point 2 and heron-ready's section 2a say *install the copy's versions*; the installer installs the lock's, as [Q-63](../OPEN-QUESTIONS.md)'s settled install half says. Neither is changed here. Which set a Store-Claude PC should end up with is the open half of row 417 and Q-63. **2026-10-18 - NOT settled by [D-135](../decisions/D-135.md)**, which moved Heron's DATA and not its packages. While the copy holds packages Claude installed, they shadow the real folder's file by file whatever Heron does with its data, so which set a Store-Claude PC should end with is the same choice it was. Reasoned, not tried: once the copy holds no Heron packages - a Reset of the app, or the owner removing them - the installer's lock versions are the only ones, and the advice to install the copy's versions (WHAT-TO-INSTALL §1b point 2, heron-ready section 2a) is the one to retire. Still Ajmal PS's. |
| **1066** | **FROM INSIDE THE STORE CLAUDE, A WRITE INTO A FOLDER THAT ALREADY EXISTS ON THE REAL DISK LANDS ON THE REAL DISK - NOT IN THE APP'S COPY - SO THE INSTALLERS' REFUSAL SAYS SOMETHING THIS LAPTOP DOES NOT DO.** Measured 2026-10-17 on the owner's laptop, Store Claude `AnthropicPBC.Claude_2.31226.1.0_x64__fnn82j28hfe8t`: a folder made under `%APPDATA%\Heron` from the owner's own terminal; from Claude's shell, a line appended to a file in it and a NEW file made in it; read back from the terminal, both were on the real disk, and the copy's `LocalCache\Roaming\Heron` held no such folder. The refusal ([row 495](section-5b-rows-401-425.md)) says *"an add-in installed from here would land where Revit never looks"*, and [Q-63](../OPEN-QUESTIONS.md) with rows 416 to 419 describes every write under AppData as sent to the copy. What row 416 measured on 2026-10-10 were writes whose folders did not yet exist outside - the packages, the store. On this laptop every `Addins\<release>` folder exists, so an install from inside would plausibly have reached Revit after all. **NOT checked:** a folder that exists in BOTH places, such as `Python\Python312\site-packages` now; a new folder made from inside; and the add-in install itself from inside - the refusal stopped it, as it should. | **OPEN - recorded, not fixed.** The refusal itself stays: from inside, where a file lands depends on which folders exist where, which nobody installing can see. A new FOLDER made from inside a real-only folder reached the real disk. So the refusal's sentence is true wherever the copy holds the folder, and which folders it holds depends on what was installed from where - the refusal stays; its wording is still the installer's session's. |
| **1067** | **`tools/check-gaps.py` NAMED `brain/heron_install.py` AS IMPORTED BY NO SUITE - ITS ONE UNFINISHED ITEM ON A PLAIN CONTAINER - WHILE [`tests/test_install_lines.py`](../../tests/test_install_lines.py) TESTS IT: THE SUITE LOADS IT BY PATH, AND THE CHECK COUNTED ONLY AN `import`.** Found 2026-10-17 running check-gaps for [pull request #628](https://github.com/Ajmalpshaik/Heron-AI/pull/628) on main `a5a349ba` and again on `2f6124ab`, in a cloud container without `model2vec`: exit 1, and the UNFINISHED list held only *"1 brain module(s) are imported by no suite at all: brain/heron_install.py"*. `brain/heron_install.py` came with [row 5b-944](section-5b-rows-401-425.md)'s change (#600), and its suite loads it through `importlib.util.spec_from_file_location` from `os.path.join(BRAIN, "heron_install.py")` on purpose, so that a missing module is one FAIL line rather than a traceback. The brain section of check-gaps counted an `import` or `from` line naming the module and nothing else, the reading row 5b-91 gave it. | **FIXED 2026-10-17 in the tool - tooling, no fragment, no Revit.** One reading, `suite_loads()` in `tools/check-gaps.py`: an import counts, and so does the module's file name as an argument to `os.path.join` in a suite that calls `spec_from_file_location`; a comment naming the file, a path with no loader and a longer module name do not. | **1068** | **THE INSTALLER WINDOW'S TOP LINE STILL SAYS *"Revit 2026 is open right now"* AFTER REVIT HAS CLOSED AND THE INSTALL HAS FINISHED.** Seen 2026-10-17 running [EP8](../needs-checking/group-ep.md) on the owner's laptop, the second run: the window opened at 23:37 while an empty Revit 2026.5 was open, Install was pressed, Revit exited at 23:38:13.8 and the run ended READY at 23:38:36.3 - and the line above the report still read *"Revit 2026 is open right now. Heron cannot replace a file that Revit has open, so it waits for you rather than working around it."* `InstallerWindow` adds `screen.CloseRevitFirst` as a notice once, when the window is built, and `InstallerScreen` worked it out from the Revits running at that moment; nothing refreshes it. NOT RUN: the line in a real window on a PC with Revit - EP9. |
| **1069** | **`tools/heron-backup.py` STILL BACKS UP `%APPDATA%\Heron` BY DEFAULT, SO SINCE D-135 THE KNOWLEDGE FOLDER AND THE BRAIN'S AUDIT - NOW IN `%USERPROFILE%\.heron` - ARE BACKED UP ONLY WHEN `HERON_DATA` NAMES THAT FOLDER.** Found 2026-10-18 while building [D-135](../decisions/D-135.md). `data_root()` answers `HERON_DATA`, else `%APPDATA%\Heron`, the one folder the backup copies and a restore puts back, and `backup_root()` is a folder under it. D-135 moved the Python side's DATA - each project's memory and the brain's audit - into a second folder, so on a PC that never sets `HERON_DATA` a backup now holds the add-in's settings and trail and none of the project memory, which it held before on a PC whose Claude is not the Store build. Also: run from inside a Store Claude, the default backup folder `%APPDATA%\Heron\backup` is in the app's copy, which a Reset deletes along with what it backs up. The manifest records each part's `source`, and a restore puts each part back into that folder and no other, refusing before it writes anything when a recorded folder's parent is not on this PC. The default backup folder is `<home>\backup`, out of a Store Claude's copy; backups already in `%APPDATA%\Heron\backup` are still listed. The drill restores each part into a scratch folder of its own name and compares both. With `HERON_DATA` set it is that folder alone, flat, as before. That you can decide."* A family is six to ten `revit_change` calls, and each put the box up and waited, so a job he walked away from stopped at its first change. `write.askBeforeKeeping` (D-126 item 5) turned the box off for every job at once and could be turned back on only in a text editor; no ribbon button read or wrote it. | **BUILT 2026-10-18 as [D-138](../decisions/D-138.md) - compiled for 2020 to 2027, NOT RUN IN REVIT.** An *Ask to Keep* button beside Changes, Admin and Publish writes the key through `HeronPermissions.SetAsksBeforeKeeping`, asks once on the way off through `HeronSwitchWindow`, never on the way on, and is OFF by default - the owner's second word the same day, *"make the box off by default"*, in `HeronConfig.cs` and `heron_config.py` both; `tools/heron-ready.py` section 7 lists it. While it is on, the box has a third link, *"Keep, and keep the rest of this job without asking"*, which keeps the change and lets `HeronKeepGrant` keep the same chat's next changes to the same model without the box, each its own undo step, until ten minutes pass with none, another chat or model asks, the switch is turned off, the model closes or Revit closes. A publish and a DRAFT's one run are always asked. `tools/time-family-build.py` counts the third link as kept. `tests/test_keep_question.py` sections 8 to 12 and `tests/Heron.KeepQuestion.TestHost` sections 7 to 10 - 43 checks red against the code as it stood, the host's build among them (it cannot build without `HeronKeepGrant.cs`), none crashing; all green after. Owed: [NEEDS-CHECKING Group HG](../needs-checking/group-hg.md), on the owner's PC. |

The negative, `Work Plane-Based=no` on a family already No, returned `changed 0`, `alreadySet 1` - and `heron_validate.py draft` still wrote *"the negative case returned content"*, because `looks_empty` reads every declared result and a filled sentence is content there; only `positive_worked` lets a count outrank a sentence (D-51). | **FIXED 2026-10-18 in the card.** `settingReport` is `role: accounting` - `changed` is the count that says whether anything changed. The fingerprint hashes the implementation only, so the run stood; re-drafted from the same record, signed by Ajmal PS and promoted the same day. **Open, not fixed here:** `looks_empty` does not apply D-51's count-before-sentence rule that `positive_worked` applies - any other card with a read-back sentence declared a result meets the same refusal. |

| **1110** | **A LONG PROVING RUN FILLS REVIT 2020'S MEMORY: AFTER ABOUT 110 `batch-prove` JOBS REVIT HELD 28 GB OF COMMITTED MEMORY, THE LAPTOP'S 63 GB COMMIT LIMIT WAS FULL, THE LAST FIVE JOBS CAME BACK `operation_failed: Insufficient memory to continue the execution of the program.` AND THE CLAUDE CHAT DRIVING THEM CRASHED.** Measured 2026-10-18 on the owner's laptop (16 GB RAM), Revit 2020.2 with the add-in deployed from `main` `16ae93d2`, two never-saved scratch documents (Project2 and the Generic Models family Family1): five batches of rolled-back proofs - 111 jobs, each two legs with up to three setup steps - over about two hours. `Get-Process Revit` then read `PagedMemorySize64` 27.6 GB against a working set of 0.9 GB, and `Win32_OperatingSystem` 63.3 of 63.4 GB committed. Read before each of five more jobs by a scratch runner, the same Revit grew 27.68 -> 29.01 GB, about 0.27 GB a job. A fresh Revit (session 2648) went 1.4 -> 10.3 GB over about 35 kept creator runs, three probes and eleven proof jobs, and to 18.3 GB by fifty jobs. Only closing Revit freed it. **Not measured: what holds the memory.** Each fragment run compiles C# inside Revit's process, and on .NET Framework a loaded assembly is never unloaded - a candidate, not a finding. The proofs that ran before the failures are sound; the five that failed were run again on the fresh Revit. | **OPEN.** Until it is found: read Revit's commit before each batch, and restart Revit (a scratch model needs no save) every 50 to 60 jobs or past about 15 GB. |

| **1150** | **EVERY CHAT ON THE OWNER'S PC SENT REVIT THE SAME ID, SO TWO CHATS ON ONE MODEL SHARED EACH OTHER'S CARRIED ELEMENTS AND KEEP GRANT, AND NOTHING STOPPED THEM WORKING ONE MODEL AT ONCE.** Found 2026-10-18 by an architecture audit, read in the source and not run. `.mcp.json` sets `HERON_CLIENT_ID = "ajmal-pc"` for every server process, on purpose, for the D-22 lease (the comment above `CLIENT_ID` in `mcp/client/heron_bridge_client.py`), and the add-in keyed one chat's state on that id: `RevitFragment.Run` read `client` as the chat, so a call without `expect_from` sent `"chain": "reset"` and `Forget(client)` wiped another chat's carried chain; `HeronKeepGrant.Decide`'s *"another chat"* branch could never fire, so a grant one chat was given kept another chat's changes and the reply said *"the same chat"*. A model is held by the chat whose request ran against it and another chat is refused `model_in_use` before anything runs (`HeronModelHold`, claimed in `RevitOperations.Run` after the gate); it frees after `bridge.modelHoldMinutes` (3) quiet minutes, on `revit_release`, when the model or Revit closes, and with **Free this model** on the ribbon. | **1153** | **A PROOF'S FINGERPRINT COVERS ONLY THE FRAGMENT'S `impl/` FOLDER, SO A PROVEN CAPABILITY STAYS KEEPABLE AFTER ITS CARD'S CONTRACT CHANGES, ON A REVIT RELEASE IT WAS NEVER PROVED ON, UNDER A CHANGED ADD-IN, AND ON A PATH ITS PROOF NEVER TOOK.** Found 2026-10-18 by the agent-architecture audit - read only, at `4192360`, nothing run in Revit. `Fragment.proof_files` hashes every file under `impl/` and nothing else (`brain/heron_fragment.py:1086-1096`; `fingerprint` at `:1098-1149`), and [D-117](../decisions/D-117.md)'s gate asks only the status and that fingerprint (`unproven_refusal`, `mcp/client/heron_bridge_client.py:1400-1442`, called at `mcp/server/heron_mcp_server.py:1833`). Four things a proof rests on are outside it. (1) **The card's contract:** `fragment.yaml`'s needs, defaults and optional flags are read again on every call (`bridge.fragment_needs`, `heron_mcp_server.py:1851`), so a changed default or a need made optional changes what runs while the proof still reads current. (2) **The Revit release:** `unproven_refusal` never compares the Revit in front with the proof's `model:` line or the card's `revit:` list, and `revit_change` does not ask `_revit_version` at all when a capability is named, so a capability proved only on 2024 is kept on 2020. (4) **The path:** `unload-links` is PROVEN on a run with no transaction open (its proof, 2026-09-14) and Revit refuses it inside one ([row 73](section-5-rows-051-075.md), measured both ways); since [row 5b-350](section-5b-rows-326-350.md) its only door is `revit_change`, which opens one. **Not checked:** how many PROVEN cards' `fragment.yaml` changed after their proof date, and whether a chat has kept a change on a release no proof names. | **OPEN - recorded, not repaired; the owner's call, because it decides what a proof means.** What a repair would be: the fingerprint takes in the card's `contract:` block; the gate refuses a release the proof's `model:` line does not name (the line `heron_matrix.proven_releases` already reads); the proof records the add-in version and the path, read or write, it ran on, and the gate compares both. Each part makes proofs stale the day it lands. |
| **1154** | **`proof_is_stale` ANSWERS "NOT STALE" FOR A CARD WITH NO PROOF AT ALL, AGAINST ITS OWN DOCSTRING - *"Unknown counts as stale"* - SO A CARD EDITED TO `heron-status: PROVEN` WITH NO `proof:` BLOCK PASSES [D-117](../decisions/D-117.md)'S GATE AT RUN TIME.** Found 2026-10-18 by the agent-architecture audit, read only. `brain/heron_fragment.py:1159-1160` returns False when `self.proof` is empty, five lines under the docstring's sentence (`:1154`). `heron_brain.proof_status` sets `stale` to `bool(proof) and frag.proof_is_stale()` (`mcp/server/heron_brain.py:2592`), so no proof reads as fresh, and `unproven_refusal` (`mcp/client/heron_bridge_client.py:1428`, `:1435`) then sees PROVEN, not stale, and lets the change be kept. **What does catch it:** `proof_problems` - *"status is PROVEN but there is no proof"* (`brain/heron_fragment.py:1672`) - through `validate`, which the store's rebuild, `check-gaps` and the suites run, so a committed card like that fails CI. The run-time gate never calls it, so an edit on a PC - by hand, or by the chat's own shell (row 5b-1155) - is not caught before a change is kept. But the host runs a shell; nothing served to a chat says not to edit that file - `brain/instructions/host.chat.yaml` does not name it, and `revit_change`'s description (`mcp/server/heron_mcp_server.py:1716-1723`) names the switches and says only *"never try another capability to get round it"*; and no hook covers it - `.claude/settings.json` guards Write and Edit for the vendor namespace and Bash for merges. | **1156** | **THE MCP SERVER RUNS THE FRAGMENTS, PROOFS AND PYTHON OF THE CHECKOUT ITS OWN FILE SITS IN, FIXED AT CHAT START - AND NOTHING A CHAT CAN ASK NAMES THAT FOLDER, ITS BRANCH OR ITS COMMIT.** Found 2026-10-18 by the agent-architecture audit, read only. Every path the server reads is `_repo_root()`, two folders up from `heron_mcp_server.py` (`mcp/server/heron_mcp_server.py:3907-3909`): `_fragment_for` (`:1229`), the source it sends (`:1838-1849`), and `heron_brain.ROOT` (`mcp/server/heron_brain.py:70`), where `proof_status` reads the proof (`:2585`). `.mcp.json` starts the server by a relative path, so a chat opened in a folder runs that folder's server - the ordinary case, and then the two agree. **They part when a session moves after it started** - a worktree entered mid-session, or a change of folder: the server is still the first folder's, so `heron_lookup`, `revit_read` and `revit_change` answer from, prove from and send the code of a checkout the session is no longer editing. `heron_version` (`:926-989`) prints Heron's version, the protocol, the SDK and each add-in - no folder, branch or commit. **Narrower than the audit's wording:** `RunningCode` watches every Heron `.py` the process loaded under `mcp/`, `brain/` and `tools/` (`mcp/server/heron_running_code.py:86`), not only the server's own file, and a card's files are read fresh on each call, so a pull into the same checkout is seen; what it cannot see is another checkout. **Not checked:** whether Claude Code restarts a project's MCP server when a session enters a worktree. | **OPEN - recorded, not repaired.** `heron_version` names the folder it serves from with that folder's branch and commit (read through git where it answers, *not read* where it does not), and a reply says so when the session's working folder and the server's differ. Developer-facing: a modeller's chat opens in one folder and stays there. |
| **1157** | **A REVIT HERON PICKED ON ITS OWN - THE ONLY ONE OPEN - IS REPLACED WITHOUT A WORD BY ANOTHER WHEN IT CLOSES, AGAINST CONSTITUTION ARTICLE 12a; [docs/25](../25-multi-session-and-binding.md) SAYS BOTH THINGS.** Found 2026-10-18 by the agent-architecture audit, read only. `SessionBinding.resolve` (`mcp/server/heron_session.py:228`): when the bound session has gone and it had been *assumed* rather than chosen, it falls through (`:274-275`, *"Never explicitly chosen ...
