If the bound session closes, stop and say so - never slide onto another one."* docs/25 says the same at line 175, and its own four-case table at line 201 says *"Assumed - only one was open / it closes / Quietly take the remaining one"*, which is what the code does. **What still stands between that slide and a wrong model:** the chat's document pin (`DocumentPin`, `mcp/server/heron_write.py:220`) refuses a different model by CreationGUID, path or title, and a write is aimed at the pinned model by name ([D-74](../decisions/D-74.md)). The pin holds no Revit process id (`_FIELDS`, `:255-257`), so the same model open in the other Revit passes, and the chat is not told it changed Revit. Read, not run. | **OPEN - a policy conflict, recorded both ways; the owner resolves it.** By [PROJECT-MAP section D](../PROJECT-MAP.md) the Constitution says what Heron may do and docs/25 explains intent, so code that follows row 201 against Article 12a is the defect unless the Article is amended. If 12a stands: the assumed case stops and says so too, and docs/25 row 201 is corrected with it. If row 201 stands: 12a carries the exception in words, and the next reply says the Revit changed. |
| **1158** | **SOME READS ANSWER LENGTHS AND AREAS IN REVIT'S INTERNAL FEET AND SQUARE FEET, AS BARE NUMBERS, AND NOTHING BETWEEN THE FRAGMENT AND THE CHAT LABELS OR CONVERTS THEM - [D-71](../decisions/D-71.md) MADE EVERY LENGTH A CALLER TYPES MILLIMETRES AND SAYS NOTHING OF WHAT COMES BACK.** Found 2026-10-18 by the agent-architecture audit, read only. `read-room-geometry` (PROVEN) leaves `area = room.Area` and `height` from the room-height parameter's `AsDouble()` (`brain/fragments/read-room-geometry/impl/any/fragment.cs:52`, `:63`), and its own proof records *"area 403.000806001612"* and *"height 8"* (`fragment.yaml`, `proof:`, 2026-09-13) - 403 square feet is about 37.4 m², and 8 feet is 2438 mm. `read-ceiling-grid` leaves `spacingX` and `spacingY` from a fill grid's `Offset` (`impl/any/fragment.cs:74-75`) and `report-level-elevations` leaves `worstDifference` from level elevations (`impl/any/fragment.cs:61`, `:74`), both in feet. `RevitFragment.Describe` prints a double as its digits (`revit/Heron.Revit.Addin/RevitFragment.cs:7053-7054`) and `_provides_lines` prints the value as it came (`mcp/server/heron_mcp_server.py:1398`), so a chat reads `area 403.000806001612` with no unit, and a modeller who works in mm and m² can be handed a number 10.76 or 304.8 times off. **Narrower than the audit's wording:** `read-ceiling-grid` and `report-level-elevations` are DRAFT on 2026-10-18, not PROVEN; of the three, only `read-room-geometry` is. **Not checked:** the rest of the library - how many results are bare internal lengths, and how many already convert. | **OPEN - recorded, not repaired.** First a sweep that lists every result declared `double` with whether its code converts; then the owner's choice between each fragment returning mm and m² under a name that says so (each PROVEN one stales its proof) and the card declaring a result's unit so the reply converts and labels it in one place. |
| **1159** | **EVERY REPLY A CHAT GETS IS PROSE: NO FIXED WORD FOR HOW A CALL ENDED, NO MCP `isError`, NO STRUCTURED CONTENT - AND THE TWO ANSWERS FOR A RUN STILL GOING CARRY NO WORKFLOW ID, WHILE ONE OF THEM SAYS *"This run is not recorded"* OF A RUN THAT WILL BE.** Found 2026-10-18 by the agent-architecture audit, read only. Every tool in `mcp/server/heron_mcp_server.py` returns a string except `revit_view_image` (read from the file's syntax tree), and nothing in `mcp/server/` names `isError` or structured content. How a call ended IS worked out - `_ended` (`:373-388`) - and given to the Companion's activity list alone (*"Display only: it never changes the answer"*, `:384`); `heron_failure.explain` prints the far side's message and a next-step sentence, never the outcome it classified (`mcp/server/heron_failure.py:472-496`, built at `:484`). So a host tells *refused*, *failed* and *done* apart by reading sentences. `awaiting_modeller` and `still_running` (`revit/Heron.Revit.Addin/RevitDispatcher.cs:386-391`, `:412-416`) come back with a code and a message and no workflow id, so the chat has nothing to hand `heron_run_record` later. And when the modeller presses Keep after the wait ran out, the job still finishes on Revit's thread, `RunRecord` writes its record under that workflow id (`:568`) and the audit line is written (`:685`) - so *"This run is not recorded"* (`:390`) is not so. Read, not run. | **OPEN - recorded, not repaired.** (1) One first line every door writes the same way - kept / refused / rolled back / outcome unknown / still running - from the outcome already computed, and `isError` on a refusal or a failure where the SDK serves it; this changes what every door test reads, so it wants the owner. (2) The workflow id on both answers, and the sentence corrected: the run is recorded when it ends, and where to read it. |
| **1160** | **IN A BATCH OF CALLS THE SAME REFUSAL A SECOND TIME IS NOT PRINTED - IT BECOMES *"and 1 value unchanged from the last reply"* - WHILE THE CODE'S OWN COMMENT SAYS A REFUSAL IS PRINTED AS EVER.** Found 2026-10-18 by the agent-architecture audit, read only. `Batch.repeated` (`mcp/server/heron_run_records.py:360-372`) returns every name whose value is word for word what the last reply of the same capability showed in the batch; `shown_values` (`:405-420`) leaves all of them out whenever the reply names a run record, and `unchanged_line` (`:423-437`) counts them on one line (`:436`). Nothing looks at the name or at what the value says, so ten `revit_change` calls refused for one reason print the refusal once and then, nine times, a line that reads as if the change went through with nothing new to say. The class docstring (`:325-329`): *"a value that differs, a refusal above all, is printed as ever"* - true of a refusal that differs, not of one that repeats. A reply names a run record for every fragment write the add-in described (`revit/Heron.Revit.Addin/RevitDispatcher.cs:568`), a refused one included. [Row 5b-407](section-5b-rows-401-425.md) built the counting. Read, not run. | **OPEN - recorded, not repaired.** A value named `refused`, and any other output the card declares as its refusals, is printed every time, however often it repeats - with a check that sends two identical refusals through one batch and fails on the code as it stands. |
| **1161** | **`revit_change` HAS A CEILING AND NO FLOOR: IT RUNS A READ, ANALYZE, EXECUTE OR SUGGEST CAPABILITY AS A WRITE, IN A TRANSACTION GROUP, BEHIND THE CHANGES SWITCH - AND *"select all ducts"* IS DECLARED ON A READ THAT SELECTS NOTHING, WHILE `revit_select_by_category` CLAIMS THE SAME WORDS.** Found 2026-10-18 by the agent-architecture audit, read only. `risk_refusal` lets every risk in `RUNNABLE_RISKS` through (`mcp/client/heron_bridge_client.py:1012`, `:1233`) and `write_operation` sends each of them as `run_fragment_write` (`:1252-1267`); `revit_change` calls both (`mcp/server/heron_mcp_server.py:1807`, `:1811`) and never `heron_tools.door_refusal`, which only `revit_read` (`:2964`) and `revit_show` (`:3157`) call. So a read asked through `revit_change` needs Changes on and a PROVEN status, runs inside a group, and can meet the Keep box - the shape [row 5b-456](section-5b-rows-401-425.md) met with `OPEN_VIEW`. `filter-elements-by-category` (`FILTER_ELEMENTS_BY_CATEGORY`, `risk: READ`, PROVEN) declares *"select all ducts"* as its first utterance (`brain/fragments/filter-elements-by-category/fragment.yaml:24`, `:174`), and its code never touches the selection; `revit_select_by_category`'s description names the same words (`heron_mcp_server.py:732`). **And five fixed tools answer what library capabilities answer, by other code:** `revit_levels` (`:5407`), `revit_worksets` (`:5515`), `revit_views` (`:5645`), `revit_sheets` (`:5906`) and `revit_isolate_temporary` (`:3302`) send the add-in's own `list_levels`, `list_worksets`, `list_views`, `list_sheets` and `isolate_temporary`, beside `LIST_LEVELS` (DRAFT), `LIST_WORKSETS`, `FIND_VIEWS`, `LIST_SHEETS` and `ISOLATE_ELEMENTS` (PROVEN) - two answers to one question, which can disagree. **Not checked:** which door a host picks for *"select all ducts"* today. | **OPEN - recorded, not repaired.** (1) `revit_change` refuses a capability below MODIFY and names the door that runs it, through `door_refusal`'s own table. (2) *"select all ducts"* moves from the READ's card to the capability that selects - a routing change, measured with `score-routing` on both trees. (3) Each fixed tool and its library twin: one goes, or one calls the other - the owner's call under [D-118](../decisions/D-118.md)'s budget. |
| **1162** | **WHAT THE HOST PASSES IS FILED AS THE MODELLER'S OWN WORD: A PROJECT'S DESIGN BASIS IS KEPT AS *"what the modeller said"*, AND AN ANSWER ABOUT EARLIER RECORDS IS STAMPED *"the modeller, in the chat"* AND NEVER ASKED AGAIN - ON NOTHING BUT AN INSTRUCTION THAT THE HOST MUST NOT ANSWER ON THE MODELLER'S BEHALF.** Found 2026-10-18 by the agent-architecture audit, read only. `heron_designbasis` keeps a project's standards as data because *"it is what the modeller said"* (`brain/heron_designbasis.py:50-53`), and `record` (`:142`) is handed every value whose source is the request (`mcp/server/heron_brain.py:1570-1581`) - the calculation's `inputs`, which the host fills. `heron_earlier.decide` defaults `by="the modeller, in the chat"` (`brain/heron_earlier.py:384`) and, on `none`, records it and *"will not ask again for this model"* (`:429-436`); `heron_earlier_answers` passes the host's `use` straight in (`mcp/server/heron_mcp_server.py:5035-5072`), and only its description says *"Never answer it yourself"* (`:5050`). An MCP server cannot see who typed a value, so the defect is the label and the finality, not the call: a host that answers by itself leaves a record saying a person decided, and the question that would have caught it is gone for that model. **Already recorded, not repeated:** answers keyed on the model's CreationGUID are shared by its copies - [row 5b-324](section-5b-rows-301-325.md) and [D-113](../decisions/D-113.md) say so (`mcp/server/heron_write.py:251-254`). Read, not run. | **OPEN - recorded, not repaired.** The record says what is known - *given in the chat* - and the reply that kept a value shows the modeller what was kept, so a wrong one is caught while it is in front of them; a `none` that will not be asked again says the same. Whether a kept answer needs a confirmation on Revit's side, as [D-126](../decisions/D-126.md) gives a change, is the owner's call. |
| **1163** | **A RUN RECORD SAYS KEPT FOR GOOD: A CHANGE THE MODELLER UNDID WITH CTRL+Z IS STILL `KEPT` IN `heron_run_record`, BECAUSE THE UNDO IS KNOWN ONLY IN THE ADD-IN'S MEMORY.** Found 2026-10-18 by the agent-architecture audit, read only. The record is written once, when the job finishes (`RunRecord`, `revit/Heron.Revit.Addin/RevitDispatcher.cs:763-782`, called at `:568`), and `heron_run_record` prints its verdict from `verdictKind` - `KEPT` for `kept` (`mcp/server/heron_run_records.py:233-247`). An undo is seen: `OnDocumentChanged` hands `TransactionUndone` and `TransactionRedone` to the rollback witness (`revit/Heron.Revit.Addin/RevitFragment.cs:3333`, `:3348`), which marks a kept job's elements as expected to go so the next job's start does not blame anything (`HeronRollbackWitness.cs:336-341`) - in memory, gone when Revit closes. Nothing writes it to the record or the audit trail, so a chat paging a record after Ctrl+Z tells the modeller the change is in the model. Read, not run. | **OPEN - recorded, not repaired; NEEDS REAL REVIT to prove.** When the witness sees a kept job's own undo entry undone or redone, one line goes on that job's record - undone at, redone at - and `heron_run_record` prints the latest; the record says in words that an undo made while the add-in was not running cannot be seen. |
| **1164** | **THE CHAIN - WHAT ONE FRAGMENT LEAVES FOR THE NEXT - IS MATCHED TO A MODEL BY ITS TITLE, A FEW HUNDRED LINES FROM THE ADD-IN'S OWN SENTENCE THAT A TITLE IS NOT A MODEL'S IDENTITY.** Found 2026-10-18 by the agent-architecture audit, read only. Binding takes the carried values only when `chain.Document == target.Title` (`revit/Heron.Revit.Addin/RevitFragment.cs:3609`), and `Remember` clears and re-stamps the chain by the same comparison (`:4048-4050`). `InDocument`'s summary (`:4103-4106`): *"a title is not: two files can share one"* - which is why each element is checked with `Document.Equals` when it is kept ([row 75](section-5-rows-051-075.md) built that). So the ids are checked against their own model when they are KEPT, and at the next step the chain only asks whether the target has the same title: ids taken in one model can be bound in another of that name and looked up there by number. **Not measured:** whether Revit lets two documents with one title be open in one session at once - the add-in's comment says they can, and `DocumentPin`'s *"a DIFFERENT one with the same name"* refusal (`mcp/server/heron_write.py:484-494`) was written for it. | **OPEN - recorded, not repaired.** The chain keeps the model it was taken in by what identifies it - the document itself, or its path and CreationGUID where Revit gives one - and compares that, as `InDocument` does; with a test-host case of two documents sharing a title. |
| **1165** | **`heron_resolve` TELLS THE CHAT *"It touches nothing"*, AND FOR A CAPABILITY NO FRAGMENT PROVIDES IT WRITES THE NAME INTO THE SHARED KNOWLEDGE STORE AS WANTED - A HOST'S TYPO INCLUDED.** Found 2026-10-18 by the agent-architecture audit, read only. The description: *"It touches nothing and needs no Revit"* (`mcp/server/heron_mcp_server.py:4330-4331`). `heron_brain.resolve` calls `CAP.want(store, capability, "asked for by name and no fragment provides it")` when no provider exists on any release (`mcp/server/heron_brain.py:572-574`), and writes an audit line either way (`:580`). The want is by decision - [D-63](../decisions/D-63.md) - so the write is not the defect; the sentence is, and so is what reaches the gap report: the name is whatever the host typed, and `capabilities_wanted` sits in the one store every chat on the machine reads, unless `HERON_KNOWLEDGE` names another. Read, not run. | **OPEN - recorded, not repaired.** The description says what is so: nothing in the model changes, and a capability nobody provides is noted as wanted (D-63). Whether a name close to nothing Heron knows should be noted at all is the gap report's question, for the owner. |
| **1166** | **`validate --write` AND `validate --allow-publish` ARE SENT AS SAFE TO REPEAT, SO AN ANSWER LOST AFTER SENDING IS SENT AGAIN - AND A PUBLISH, AN EXPORT TO DISK, IS NOT UNDONE BY THE GROUP'S ROLLBACK.** Found 2026-10-18 by the agent-architecture audit, read only. `cmd_validate` (`mcp/client/heron_bridge_client.py:2290`) calls `bridge.request(write_op if writing else "run_fragment_read", ...)` with no `idempotent` (`:2766-2767`), so the default, True (`:389`), applies, and `request` sends again after an answer lost once the request had gone (`:459-477`), up to `RETRY_ATTEMPTS`, 3 (`:252`) - against its own docstring, *"Step 6 writes MUST pass False"* (`:406`). `write_op` is `write_operation`'s answer (`:2456`), so `run_fragment_publish` or `run_fragment_admin` under `--allow-publish`. A MODIFY run is rolled back, so a repeat leaves nothing in the model unless the rollback does not hold (section 1c); a PUBLISH fragment's export is a file outside the model, and a second run writes it again. [Row 5b-273](section-5b-rows-251-275.md) set this right in `cmd_fragment` and both chat doors, and did not reach this call. Read, not run. | **OPEN - recorded, not repaired.** `idempotent=not writing` on that call, as row 5b-273 did for `cmd_fragment`, with a check that fails on the code as it stands. |
| **1167** | **NOTHING CAPS THE SIZE OF AN ANSWER ON THE PIPE, AND A READ WHOSE ANSWER WAS LOST OR WOULD NOT PARSE IS CALLED *"never ran"* AND *"asking again costs nothing"*.** Found 2026-10-18 by the agent-architecture audit, read only. The bridge writes whatever the operation returned as one line (`revit/Heron.Bridge/BridgeServer.cs:590`); the client reads 4096-byte chunks until a newline with no limit (`mcp/client/heron_bridge_client.py:355-361`, `buffer += chunk`) and holds all of it before it parses. [Row 5b-489](section-5b-rows-401-425.md) met a 6.2 MB record from one read. A reply that arrived and would not parse comes back `None` (`:482-485`), the same as no reply, and `heron_failure` classifies `None` on a read as `no_reply`, `NEVER_RAN`, `RETRY` - *"the request only reads, so asking again costs nothing"* (`mcp/server/heron_failure.py:442-444`). For a read that ran it is not free: one that used up the chain ([row 5b-273](section-5b-rows-251-275.md)), or one the add-in counted changing the model ([row 5b-350](section-5b-rows-326-350.md)). **Not measured:** the largest reply a real read has sent over the pipe. | **OPEN - recorded, not repaired.** A cap at both ends, named in one place, with an answer over it refused in a sentence that says how big it was and that the run record holds it; and an unparseable reply kept apart from no reply, classified as an unknown outcome, because it arrived, so the request ran. |
| **1168** | **IN A CHAT OPENED IN A GIT CHECKOUT - HOW THE OWNER WORKS AS A MODELLER - DEVELOPER FILES LOAD BESIDE HERON'S TOOLS; AND THE TOOL DESCRIPTIONS EVERY CHAT IS GIVEN SIT 11 CHARACTERS UNDER THEIR BUDGET.** Found 2026-10-18 by the agent-architecture audit, read only. `.mcp.json` belongs to the project, so Heron starts only in a chat opened in the Heron folder, and in a git checkout that folder also holds `AGENTS.md` (read by Claude Code when there is no `CLAUDE.md`), `.claude/settings.json` - four hooks: the session-start line about branches and fragment counts, a check on every prompt, the vendor-namespace guard on every edit and the main-moved check on every Bash call - and the developer skills and agents under `.claude/`. `heron-guard`'s and `heron-session`'s own descriptions say each is *"not part of what a modeller installs"* (`.claude/skills/heron-guard/SKILL.md:3`, `.claude/skills/heron-session/SKILL.md:3`). **Narrower than the audit's wording:** the release download carries neither `.claude/` nor `AGENTS.md` (`WORKSPACE_KEEP`, `tools/build-release-assets.py:270-284`), so a modeller who installed from it is not affected; [row 5b-378](section-5b-rows-376-400.md) met the `AGENTS.md` half on a new laptop and put a modeller's paragraph at its top. **The budget:** `python tests/test_tool_sizes.py` on 2026-10-18 measured the six always-loaded tools' descriptions at 4,489 characters of `ALWAYS_LOADED_BUDGET`, 4,500 (`tests/test_tool_sizes.py:80`), and `revit_change`'s at 1,460 of the 1,500 one tool may have (`:86`) - so the next sentence any of them needs fails the gate, and the pull is to cut words a host relies on. Not part of this row: the served instructions' own 4,096-character cut, which is another task's. | **OPEN - recorded, not repaired; the owner's call.** (1) On the owner's PCs modelling and development share one folder: either the hooks stand down when no development is going on, or modelling uses a release folder and development the checkout. (2) The budgets are a decision ([D-118](../decisions/D-118.md)): re-measure what each always-loaded description carries, or raise a budget on purpose, before the next needed sentence is cut to fit. |
| **1169** | **`split-register` REFUSES TO SPLIT THE REGISTER ONCE A ROW LINKS TO A 5b ROW NUMBERED 1000 OR MORE: IT NAMES A ROWS FILE WITH `%03d`, WHICH GIVES FOUR DIGITS THERE, AND COMPARES ITS READ-BACK WITH A PATTERN THAT TAKES EXACTLY THREE.** Found 2026-10-18 writing rows 5b-1153 to 5b-1168 - in passing, so recorded, not repaired (AGENTS.md). `name_of` builds a band's file as `"%s-%03d-%03d.md"` (`tools/split-register.py:297`), so rows 1151 to 1175 would go to `section-5b-rows-1151-1175.md`; a link that names a moved row is re-pointed at that file, and the read-back is compared after `_band_blind`, which writes every rows-file name alike only when it matches `-[0-9]{3}-[0-9]{3}[.]md` (`:464`), so the four-digit name is left as it is and the comparison at `:719` fails. **Reproduced 2026-10-18 in a cloud container**, dry run each time: with this change's two in-register links to rows 5b-1154 and 5b-1155 in place, `python tools/split-register.py fragment-issues` exited 1 - *"REFUSED - nothing was written: the new files, read back, differ from the register in more than the rows file a link names"*; with those two written as plain text, it exited 0 and said the files read back byte for byte. On `main` at `4192360` it also exits 0: no row of the register links to a four-digit row yet, though `docs/04`, `docs/21` and `docs/40` do, and those are re-pointed by another path. **And `tools/archive-fragment-issues.py` asks the same function** (`split._band_blind`, `tools/archive-fragment-issues.py:593`), so it is expected to refuse the same way - read, not run. | **OPEN - recorded, not repaired.** `_band_blind` takes three or more digits on each side, `[0-9]{3,}`, with a suite case that splits a register holding a link to a four-digit row and fails on the code as it stands.
